    36	/**
    37	 * Announce that a PLATFORM ROUTER is about to run unpinned, with the provider
    38	 * ids needed to fix it. No-op for any vendor that does not declare itself one.
    39	 *
    40	 * A platform router (pi) serves users on gpt / claude / kimi / qwen / glm…, so
    41	 * hopper must not guess a model for it — but running unannounced is how a swarm
    42	 * panelist ran on `gpt-5.5` out of ~/.pi/agent/settings.json while every other
    43	 * dispatch in that project used the 5.6 line, with `requested_selector: null`
    44	 * and nothing to show it. Warn rather than refuse (operator decision
    45	 * 2026-08-11): a refusal would break every existing unpinned dispatch, and the
    46	 * run is still attested after the fact by `observed_models`.
    47	 *
    48	 * The ids are load-bearing, not decoration: pi rejects every intuitive name
    49	 * (`kimi`, `qwen`, `gemini`, `claude`, `grok` → `provider_not_found`), so a
    50	 * caller told only "pin a model" cannot act on it.
    51	 *
    52	 * @param {string[]} notices  collected policyNotices, appended in place
    53	 * @param {string} vendor
    54	 */
    55	function pushUnpinnedPlatformNotice(notices, vendor) {
    56	  let providers;
    57	  try { providers = getAdapter(vendor)?.capabilities?.modelArg?.platformProviders; } catch (_) { return; }
    58	  if (!Array.isArray(providers) || providers.length === 0) return;
    59	  notices.push(
    60	    `NOT PINNED: '${vendor}' is a multi-provider platform and hopper does not guess a model for it — `
    61	    + `this dispatch runs on whatever ${vendor} picks itself, and only the recorded observed_models will `
    62	    + 'say which. Settle it once in .hopper/AGENTS.md `## Approved Vendors` → `Default model` '
    63	    + `(or HOPPER_${String(vendor).toUpperCase()}_MODEL), as \`<provider>/<model>\`. Provider ids: `
    64	    + `${providers.map((p) => p.id).join(', ')} — \`hopper-dispatch --capabilities ${vendor}\` maps each `
    65	    + 'to its vendor and auth method (the intuitive names are rejected).',
    66	  );
    67	}
    68	
    69	/** Raw Effort policy / Model rule cells for a task-type, or the unbound-shaped default. */
    70	function policyForTaskType(agentsData, taskType) {
    71	  return (agentsData && agentsData.policies && agentsData.policies[taskType]) || { effortPolicy: '', modelRule: '' };
    72	}
    73	
    74	/**
    75	 * Resolve a task for dispatch (Phase 1 stops here; Phase 2 calls vendor adapter).
    76	 *
    77	 * @param {object} args
    78	 * @param {string} args.hopperDir          Path to .hopper/ directory
    79	 * @param {string} args.taskId             Task ID to dispatch
    80	 * @returns {Promise<{
    81	 *   task: import('./types.js').TaskRow,
    82	 *   frame: string,
    83	 *   vendor: string,
    84	 *   composedPrompt: string,
    85	 *   taskSpec: string,
    86	 *   specNotice: string|null
    87	 * }>}
    88	 */
    89	export async function resolveDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride = null }) {
    90	  // 1. Read queue.md, find task by ID
    91	  const queuePath = join(hopperDir, 'queue.md');
    92	  const tasks = await parseQueue(queuePath);
    93	  const { task, reason } = findEligibleTask(tasks, taskId);
    94	  if (!task) {
    95	    throw new Error(`Task not eligible: ${reason}`);
    96	  }
    97	
    98	  // 2. Load task-type frame
    99	  const frame = await loadTaskFrame(hopperDir, task.taskType);
   100	
   101	  // 3. Resolve vendor via AGENTS.md (deterministic, no retry state)
   102	  const agentsPath = join(hopperDir, 'AGENTS.md');
   103	  const agentsData = await parseAgentsFile(agentsPath);
   104	  // --vendor override wins over the AGENTS.md routing tables; host != vendor and
   105	  // unknown-vendor checks still apply downstream (the dispatcher validates both).
   106	  const vendor = vendorOverride || resolveVendor(task, agentsData);
   107	  // Project-level Approved Vendors whitelist (batch 3, TH-approved-vendors):
   108	  // enforced AFTER vendor resolution so a --vendor override is checked too,
   109	  // not just AGENTS.md-routed dispatches. Independent of, and does not
   110	  // short-circuit, the host!=vendor isomorphism guard applied downstream.
   111	  assertVendorApproved(agentsData, vendor);
   112	
   113	  // 4. Build the task CONTENT: the detailed spec section from
   114	  //    handoffs/leader-tasklist.md (when there is one) MERGED with the queue.md
   115	  //    Brief cell — never one silently standing in for the other.
   116	  //
   117	  //    WHY MERGE (and not "pick one"): the execution-mode guardrail composed into
   118	  //    every handoff states, verbatim, "The brief and Task spec below are the
   119	  //    complete, closed loop." (cli/src/tasks.js). Dropping either half would make
   120	  //    that sentence false for the vendor reading it — the very class of defect
   121	  //    this path used to have: loadTaskSpec's two miss branches returned a
   122	  //    PLACEHOLDER STRING claiming "using queue.md brief only" which then became
   123	  //    the spec, so the brief never reached the vendor at all and the vendor got a
   124	  //    frame with no task in it (and still exited 0 / status done).
   125	  const { taskSpec, specNotice } = await composeTaskContent({ hopperDir, taskId, task });
   126	
   127	  // 5. Resolve optional governance overlay (keyed on the resolved vendor) and
   128	  // compose. resolveGovernance is pure file I/O — no subprocess (spec §3 #4).
   129	  const governance = await resolveGovernance({ hopperDir, vendor, task });
   130	  const composedPrompt = composePrompt(frame, taskSpec, { governance });
   131	
   132	  // Batch 2: raw Effort policy / Model rule cells for this task-type, consumed by
   133	  // resolveAdapterOptsForTask's --reasoning / --model fallback chains below.
   134	  const policy = policyForTaskType(agentsData, task.taskType);
   135	
   136	  return { task, frame, vendor, composedPrompt, taskSpec, specNotice, policy, vendorDefaultModel: vendorDefaultModel(agentsData, vendor) };
   137	}
   138	
   139	/**
   140	 * Resolve an AD-HOC dispatch — a one-off task with NO queue.md row; the brief IS
   141	 * the spec. Used by the directed commands (/hopper:review|research|market) and the
   142	 * swarm so they need not author (and pollute) queue.md. Returns the same shape as
   143	 * resolveDispatch, so the caller's single-spawn / host!=vendor guarantees apply
   144	 * identically. Read-only/web-search task-type defaults still come from
   145	 * resolveAdapterOptsForTask downstream.
   146	 *
   147	 * @param {object} args
   148	 * @param {string} args.hopperDir
   149	 * @param {string} args.taskType        a scaffolded task-type (its frame must exist)
   150	 * @param {string} args.brief           the task brief (becomes the spec)
   151	 * @param {string} args.id              the synthetic task-id (for output files)
   152	 * @param {string|null} [args.vendorOverride]
   153	 * @returns {Promise<{task: object, frame: string, vendor: string, composedPrompt: string, taskSpec: string}>}
   154	 */
   155	export async function resolveAdhocDispatch({ hopperDir, taskType, brief, id, vendorOverride = null }) {
   156	  validateTaskId(id);
   157	  if (typeof taskType !== 'string' || !TASK_TYPE_PATTERN.test(taskType)) {
   158	    throw new Error(`Invalid --task-type "${taskType}" (expected lowercase like prd-research / code-review-acceptance).`);
   159	  }
   160	  if (typeof brief !== 'string' || brief.trim().length === 0) {
   161	    throw new Error('Ad-hoc dispatch requires a non-empty --brief.');
   162	  }
   163	  // Frame must exist for the task-type (same loader as the queued path).
   164	  const frame = await loadTaskFrame(hopperDir, taskType);
   165	  const task = { id, taskType, brief, status: 'pending', depends: [] };  // shape parity with queue TaskRow
   166	  const agentsData = await parseAgentsFile(join(hopperDir, 'AGENTS.md'));
   167	  const vendor = vendorOverride || resolveVendor(task, agentsData);
   168	  if (!vendor) {
   169	    throw new Error(`No vendor resolved for ad-hoc task-type "${taskType}". Pass --vendor <name> (no AGENTS.md preference found).`);
   170	  }
   171	  // Approved Vendors whitelist — same enforcement as resolveDispatch's queue path,
   172	  // covering the ad-hoc path (/hopper:review|research|market, swarm panelists).
   173	  assertVendorApproved(agentsData, vendor);
   174	  const taskSpec = brief;
   175	  const governance = await resolveGovernance({ hopperDir, vendor, task });
   176	  const composedPrompt = composePrompt(frame, taskSpec, { governance });
   177	  const policy = policyForTaskType(agentsData, taskType);
   178	  // specNotice: shape parity with resolveDispatch. Always null here — an ad-hoc
   179	  // dispatch has no leader-tasklist lookup to miss (the brief IS the spec, and an
   180	  // empty one was already rejected above).
   181	  return { task, frame, vendor, composedPrompt, taskSpec, specNotice: null, policy, vendorDefaultModel: vendorDefaultModel(agentsData, vendor) };
   182	}
   183	
   184	/**
   185	 * Plan a multi-vendor SWARM (panel) — fan the SAME qualitative brief out to N vendors,
   186	 * each as its own ad-hoc dispatch (one single-spawn per panelist; N tasks, not N retries).
   187	 * PURE: validates + returns the per-panelist plan; the caller dispatches each via the
   188	 * normal ad-hoc path. Restricted to READ-ONLY/qualitative task-types — swarming a write
   189	 * task would have N vendors edit the same files. The vendor *selection* + per-vendor config
   190	 * is a host-side confirmation gate; this only executes the confirmed list.
   191	 *
   192	 * @param {object} args
   193	 * @param {string} args.taskType   a read-only/qualitative task-type (review/research/audit)
   194	 * @param {string} args.brief
   195	 * @param {string[]|string} args.vendors   panelists (array or comma-separated)
   196	 * @param {string} [args.idBase]
   197	 * @returns {Array<{ vendor: string, id: string, taskType: string, brief: string }>}
   198	 */
   199	export function planSwarm({ taskType, brief, vendors, idBase, now = Date.now() }) {
   200	  if (typeof taskType !== 'string' || !TASK_TYPE_PATTERN.test(taskType)) {
   201	    throw new Error(`Invalid --task-type "${taskType}".`);
   202	  }
   203	  if (!READ_ONLY_DEFAULT_TASK_TYPES.includes(taskType)) {
   204	    throw new Error(`--swarm only supports read-only/qualitative task-types (${READ_ONLY_DEFAULT_TASK_TYPES.join(', ')}); got "${taskType}". Swarming a write task would have N vendors edit the same files.`);
   205	  }
   206	  if (typeof brief !== 'string' || brief.trim().length === 0) {
   207	    throw new Error('--swarm requires a non-empty --brief.');
   208	  }
   209	  const list = (Array.isArray(vendors) ? vendors : String(vendors || '').split(','))
   210	    .map((v) => v.trim()).filter(Boolean);
   211	  const uniq = [...new Set(list)];
   212	  if (uniq.length < 2) {
   213	    throw new Error('--swarm needs at least 2 vendors (--vendors v1,v2,...). Use --adhoc for a single vendor.');
   214	  }
   215	  for (const v of uniq) {
   216	    if (!VENDOR_PATTERN.test(v)) throw new Error(`Invalid vendor name "${v}" in --vendors.`);
   217	  }
   218	  const base = idBase || `swarm-${taskType}-${now.toString(36)}`;
   219	  validateTaskId(base);
   220	  return uniq.map((vendor) => ({ vendor, id: `${base}-${vendor}`, taskType, brief }));
   221	}
   222	
   223	/** Absolute path of the optional detailed-spec file. Single source of truth. */
   224	export function leaderTasklistPath(hopperDir) {
   225	  return join(hopperDir, 'handoffs', 'leader-tasklist.md');
   226	}
   227	
   228	/**
   229	 * Load the DETAILED spec section for a task from .hopper/handoffs/leader-tasklist.md.
   230	 *
   231	 * Returns the section text, or `null` when there is no detailed spec — either
   232	 * because the file has no section for this task-id, or because the file does not
   233	 * exist at all. It NEVER returns prose describing its own failure: this function
   234	 * used to return placeholder strings like
   235	 *   "(no detailed spec found for T-1 in leader-tasklist.md; using queue.md brief only)"
   236	 * which the caller then handed to composePrompt AS the task spec — so the vendor
   237	 * received a handoff whose entire "Task spec" section was a sentence about a
   238	 * missing file, the queue.md Brief was never composed in at all, and the sentence
   239	 * itself was false ("using queue.md brief only" while using nothing). Missing data
   240	 * is now reported as absence (`null`); the caller decides the fallback.
   241	 *
   242	 * Any non-ENOENT I/O error still throws — an unreadable/permission-denied
   243	 * leader-tasklist.md is a real fault and must not be laundered into "no spec".
   244	 *
   245	 * @param {string} hopperDir
   246	 * @param {string} taskId
   247	 * @returns {Promise<string|null>}  section text, or null when there is none
   248	 */
   249	export async function loadTaskSpec(hopperDir, taskId) {
   250	  const path = leaderTasklistPath(hopperDir);
   251	  let content;
   252	  try {
   253	    content = await readFile(path, 'utf-8');
   254	  } catch (err) {
   255	    if (err.code === 'ENOENT') return null;
   256	    throw err;
   257	  }
   258	  // Find a section starting with **<task-id>** or ## <task-id> or ### <task-id>
   259	  const escapedId = taskId.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
   260	  const sectionStart = content.search(new RegExp(
   261	    `(\\*\\*${escapedId}\\*\\*|^##+\\s+${escapedId}\\b|^\\|\\s*${escapedId}\\s*\\|)`,
   262	    'm'
   263	  ));
   264	  if (sectionStart === -1) return null;
   265	  // Take the next ~80 lines as the task spec (or until next H2 heading)
   266	  const rest = content.slice(sectionStart);
   267	  const nextH2 = rest.slice(50).search(/^##\s+/m);
   268	  const end = nextH2 === -1 ? Math.min(rest.length, 8000) : 50 + nextH2;
   269	  const section = rest.slice(0, end).trim();
   270	  // A section that exists but is whitespace-only is no spec either.
   271	  return section.length > 0 ? section : null;
   272	}
   273	
   274	/** Header that labels the queue.md Brief inside a merged task spec. */
   275	export const QUEUE_BRIEF_HEADING = '### Queue brief';
   276	
   277	/**
   278	 * Precedence line appended to a MERGED spec. The vendor sees two sources of task
   279	 * content; it must be told which wins, or a stale Brief cell can silently
   280	 * out-argue the detailed spec it was supposed to summarize.
   281	 */
   282	export const QUEUE_BRIEF_PRECEDENCE_NOTE =
   283	  '(Source: the Brief column of .hopper/queue.md. Where it conflicts with the detailed spec above, the detailed spec wins.)';
   284	
   285	/**
   286	 * Assemble the task CONTENT for a queued dispatch out of its two possible
   287	 * sources, and say (to the operator, never to the vendor) when one is missing.
   288	 *
   289	 * Rules:
   290	 *   - both present  → detailed spec first, then the Brief under `### Queue brief`,
   291	 *                     with an explicit "detailed spec wins on conflict" line
   292	 *   - spec only     → the spec verbatim
   293	 *   - brief only    → the brief verbatim (mirrors the ad-hoc path, where the brief
   294	 *                     IS the spec) + an operator notice explaining why there is no
   295	 *                     detailed section
   296	 *   - neither       → THROW (fail closed). Dispatching a content-free prompt is how
   297	 *                     a vendor "succeeds" (exit 0, status done) at nothing. Matches
   298	 *                     the ad-hoc/swarm paths, which already reject an empty brief.
   299	 *
   300	 * Note `??` is unusable here: queue.js gives a missing Brief cell the EMPTY STRING,
   301	 * and `'' ?? brief` is `''` — nullish coalescing only catches null/undefined.
   302	 * Everything below is emptiness-aware via .trim().
   303	 *
   304	 * @param {{hopperDir: string, taskId: string, task: {brief?: string}}} args
   305	 * @returns {Promise<{taskSpec: string, specNotice: string|null}>}
   306	 */
   307	async function composeTaskContent({ hopperDir, taskId, task }) {
   308	  const detailed = (await loadTaskSpec(hopperDir, taskId) || '').trim();
   309	  const brief = typeof task?.brief === 'string' ? task.brief.trim() : '';
   310	
   311	  if (detailed && brief) {
   312	    return {
   313	      taskSpec: `${detailed}\n\n${QUEUE_BRIEF_HEADING}\n\n${brief}\n\n${QUEUE_BRIEF_PRECEDENCE_NOTE}`,
   314	      specNotice: null,
   315	    };
   316	  }
   317	  if (detailed) return { taskSpec: detailed, specNotice: null };
   318	
   319	  // No detailed section. Distinguish "file has no entry for this id" from "no file
   320	  // at all" — same two situations the old placeholder strings described, except the
   321	  // text now goes to the OPERATOR (stderr / --resolve echo) instead of being fed to
   322	  // the vendor as if it were the task.
   323	  const path = leaderTasklistPath(hopperDir);
   324	  const tasklistExists = await fileExists(path);
   325	  if (brief) {
   326	    return {
   327	      taskSpec: brief,
   328	      specNotice: tasklistExists
   329	        ? `No detailed spec section for ${taskId} in leader-tasklist.md; task content comes from queue.md Brief.`
   330	        : `leader-tasklist.md is absent at ${path}; task content comes from queue.md Brief.`,
   331	    };
   332	  }
   333	  throw new Error(
   334	    `Task ${taskId} has no task content: queue.md Brief is empty and no detailed spec was found`
   335	    + (tasklistExists ? ` (no section for ${taskId} in ${path})` : ` (${path} does not exist)`)
   336	    + `. Fill the Brief cell for ${taskId} in .hopper/queue.md, or add a "## ${taskId}" section to leader-tasklist.md.`
   337	  );
   338	}
   339	
   340	async function fileExists(path) {
   341	  try {
   342	    await access(path);
   343	    return true;
   344	  } catch (_) {
   345	    return false;
   346	  }
   347	}
   348	
   349	/**
   350	 * Status summary for --status command.
   351	 *
   352	 * @param {string} hopperDir
   353	 */
   354	export async function getStatus(hopperDir) {
   355	  const queuePath = join(hopperDir, 'queue.md');
   356	  const tasks = await parseQueue(queuePath);
   357	  return summarizeQueue(tasks);
   358	}
   359	
   360	/**
   361	 * Return true only when the queue brief or detailed task spec explicitly says
   362	 * the task is read-only. We intentionally do not infer read-only from task-type
   363	 * names like "review"; the product default is full vendor write access unless
   364	 * the task description itself says read-only / 只读.
   365	 *
   366	 * @param {object} resolved
   367	 */
   368	export function taskTextRequestsReadOnly(resolved) {
   369	  const text = [
   370	    resolved?.task?.brief,
   371	    resolved?.taskSpec,
   372	  ].filter(Boolean).join('\n');
   373	  if (!text) return false;
   374	  if (NEGATED_READ_ONLY_RE.test(text)) return false;
   375	  return READ_ONLY_TASK_RE.test(text);
   376	}
   377	
   378	/**
   379	 * Apply the product-level default permission policy to adapter opts.
   380	 * Explicit --sandbox always wins; otherwise read-only text downgrades, and all
   381	 * other tasks default to danger-full-access.
   382	 *
   383	 * @param {object} resolved
   384	 * @param {import('./types.js').AdapterOpts} [adapterOpts]
   385	 * @returns {import('./types.js').AdapterOpts}
   386	 */
   387	export function resolveAdapterOptsForTask(resolved, adapterOpts = {}) {
   388	  const out = { ...adapterOpts };
   389	  const taskType = resolved?.task?.taskType;
   390	  // Preserve argv provenance before policy/sentinel resolution and advisory
   391	  // selector normalization mutate out.model. Runtime attestation must compare
   392	  // only the effective selector, never this requested audit value.
   393	  const requestedSelector = Object.hasOwn(adapterOpts, 'requestedSelector')
   394	    ? (typeof adapterOpts.requestedSelector === 'string' ? adapterOpts.requestedSelector : null)
   395	    : (typeof adapterOpts.model === 'string' ? adapterOpts.model : null);
   396	  const carriesEffectiveSelector = Object.hasOwn(adapterOpts, 'effectiveSelector');
   397	  const inheritedEffectiveSelector = carriesEffectiveSelector && typeof adapterOpts.effectiveSelector === 'string'
   398	    ? adapterOpts.effectiveSelector
   399	    : null;
   400	  const inheritedEffectiveSource = adapterOpts.effectiveSelectorSource;
   401	  let modelResolvedByPolicy = false;
   402	  // Batch 2: notices surfaced by the fallback chains below (policy-cell resolution,
   403	  // sentinel resolution, effort clamp visibility) — collected here and read by the
   404	  // CLI print layer as `effectiveAdapterOpts.policyNotices` immediately after this
   405	  // call. Deliberately attached NON-ENUMERABLE so it is invisible to JSON.stringify
   406	  // (background.js forwards adapterOpts to the runner via an env-JSON blob) and to
   407	  // any `{ ...effectiveAdapterOpts, ... }` spread (background/sync build effectiveOpts
   408	  // that way) — this is print-time metadata, not a real adapter option.
   409	  const notices = [];
   410	  Object.defineProperty(out, 'policyNotices', { value: notices, enumerable: false, configurable: true });
   411	
   412	  // ── --model fallback chain (batch 2): flag > AGENTS.md Model rule cell > vendor CLI default ──
   413	  // V4 normalizes a user-specified model to the vendor's canonical name (fuzzy-match
   414	  // against knownGood; advisory — passthrough if no confident match). Single
   415	  // chokepoint — every dispatch path (sync / background / adhoc / swarm) flows
   416	  // through resolveAdapterOptsForTask.
   417	  if (!out.model && !carriesEffectiveSelector && resolved?.policy?.modelRule) {
   418	    const parsedRule = parseModelRuleCell(resolved.policy.modelRule);
   419	    if (parsedRule.status === 'ok') {
   420	      out.model = parsedRule.sentinel; // resolved to a real name below (sentinel-or-normalize)
   421	      modelResolvedByPolicy = true;
   422	      notices.push(`model resolved from AGENTS.md Model rule (task-type '${taskType}'): ${parsedRule.sentinel}`);
   423	    } else if (parsedRule.status === 'unparseable') {
   424	      notices.push(`Model rule cell for task-type '${taskType}' references an unrecognized sentinel ('${String(resolved.policy.modelRule).trim()}') — ignoring; vendor CLI default will be used.`);
   425	    }
   426	    // status 'unbound' (empty / OOB `(bind per project)`) is silent — same convention
   427	    // as an unbound Default-vendor cell; falls through to the vendor's own default.
   428	  }
   429	  // ── per-vendor default (2026-08-11) ──
   430	  // Reached only when nothing more specific pinned a model. Two more levels
   880	
   881	/**
   882	 * Parse argv into task-id + bare flags + value flags.
   883	 * Skips meta-flags (--help, --version, --resolve, etc.) that main() handles
   884	 * before reaching this point. Value flags consume the next argv element;
   885	 * bare flags don't.
   886	 */
   887	function parseDispatchArgs(args) {
   888	  const META_FLAGS = new Set(['--help', '-h', '--version', '-v', '--smoke', '--status',
   889	    '--task-types', '--vendors', '--rules', '--resolve', '--watch', '--progress', '--watch-events', '--replay', '--jobs', '--reap', '--stop', '--archive',
   890	    '--check', '--capabilities', '--check-model', '--probe', '--models', '--setup', '--doctor', '--init-tasks', '--init-governance']);
   891	  const VALUE_FLAGS = new Set(ALLOWED_DISPATCH_VALUE_FLAGS);
   892	  const BARE_FLAGS = new Set(ALLOWED_DISPATCH_FLAGS);
   893	
   894	  const bareFlags = [];
   895	  const valueFlags = {};
   896	  let taskId = null;
   897	
   898	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
   899	    const arg = args[i];
   900	
   901	    if (META_FLAGS.has(arg)) continue;  // handled by main() before parseDispatchArgs
   902	
   903	    if (VALUE_FLAGS.has(arg)) {
   904	      const v = args[i + 1];
   905	      if (v === undefined || v.startsWith('-')) {
   906	        throw new Error(`${arg} requires a value (got: ${v === undefined ? 'nothing' : v}).`);
   907	      }
   908	      // Strip leading "--" for the opt key: --model → model
   909	      const key = arg.replace(/^--/, '');
   910	      valueFlags[key] = v;
   911	      i++;  // consume the value
   912	      continue;
   913	    }
   914	
   915	    if (BARE_FLAGS.has(arg)) {
   916	      bareFlags.push(arg);
   917	      continue;
   918	    }
   919	
   920	    if (arg.startsWith('-')) {
   921	      throw new Error(`Unknown flag "${arg}". Bare flags allowed: ${[...BARE_FLAGS].join(', ')}. Value flags: ${[...VALUE_FLAGS].join(', ')}.`);
   922	    }
   923	
   924	    // First non-flag, non-value arg becomes task-id
   925	    if (taskId === null) {
   926	      taskId = arg;
   927	    } else {
   928	      throw new Error(`Unexpected extra positional argument "${arg}" (task-id already set to "${taskId}").`);
   929	    }
   930	  }
   931	
   932	  return { taskId, bareFlags, valueFlags };
   933	}
   934	
   935	async function runResolve(hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride = null) {
   936	  try {
   937	    // docs/archive/ISSUES.md#resolve-ignores-vendor-override fix: thread vendorOverride straight
   938	    // into resolveDispatch — the SAME `vendorOverride || resolveVendor(...)`
   939	    // formula and the SAME assertVendorApproved(agentsData, vendor) gate
   940	    // (cli/src/dispatch.js:72,77) that a real dispatch applies, not a
   941	    // reimplementation. An override is therefore checked against
   942	    // .hopper/AGENTS.md's Approved Vendors table here exactly as it would be at
   943	    // real dispatch time (E_APPROVED_VENDORS_SECTION_MISSING / E_VENDOR_NOT_APPROVED
   944	    // surface via the catch below, same as runDispatch/runBackgroundDispatch).
   945	    const result = await resolveDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride });
   946	
   947	    // Host != vendor family isomorphism guard — runDispatch/runBackgroundDispatch
   948	    // apply this immediately after resolveDispatch, before any adapter/spawn work.
   949	    // It is pure (env lookup + a static family table; no subprocess), so a dry
   950	    // run can and must apply it too — otherwise --resolve could report a Vendor:
   951	    // line a real dispatch would actually refuse. A same-family collision throws
   952	    // (caught below, same as a real dispatch would abort); an unrecognized host
   953	    // is non-blocking and surfaces as a notice line, matching runDispatch.
   954	    const { id: hostVendor } = resolveHostIdentity();
   955	    const hostCheck = validateHostVendorSeparation(hostVendor, result.vendor);
   956	
   957	    console.log(`hopper-dispatch v${VERSION} — resolved dispatch for ${taskId}`);
   958	    console.log(`  Task-type: ${result.task.taskType}`);
   959	    console.log(`  Vendor:    ${result.vendor}${vendorOverride ? ' (--vendor override)' : ''}`);
   960	    if (hostCheck.notice) console.log(`  notice:    ${hostCheck.notice}`);
   961	    // Where the task content came from, when it did NOT come from a detailed
   962	    // leader-tasklist.md section. This diagnostic used to be smuggled into the
   963	    // vendor prompt as the task spec itself; it is an OPERATOR notice.
   964	    if (result.specNotice) console.log(`  notice:    ${result.specNotice}`);
   965	    console.log(`  Brief:     ${result.task.brief}`);
   966	    console.log(`  Composed prompt length: ${result.composedPrompt.length} chars`);
   967	    if (process.env.HOPPER_DEBUG) {
   968	      console.log('');
   969	      console.log('=== COMPOSED PROMPT ===');
   970	      console.log(result.composedPrompt);
   971	      console.log('=== END PROMPT ===');
   972	    }
   973	
   974	    // HOPPER-1: --resolve used to print the resolved vendor verbatim even when
   975	    // it is NOT a registered adapter — e.g. a model id like `gpt-5.5` mistakenly
   976	    // placed in the queue.md Vendor column or the AGENTS.md preference table.
   977	    // The failure then surfaced much later at dispatch as the opaque
   978	    // "No vendor adapter registered for 'gpt-5.5'". Catch it at resolve time and
   979	    // distinguish a model-in-the-Vendor-column mistake from a typo'd adapter.
   980	    const known = listAdapters();
   981	    if (!known.includes(result.vendor)) {
   982	      console.error('');
   983	      reportUnregisteredVendor(result.vendor, result.task, known);
   984	      process.exit(1);
   985	    }
   986	  } catch (err) {
   987	    console.error(`Error: ${err.message}`);
   988	    process.exit(1);
   989	  }
   990	}
   991	// runResolve's dry run deliberately does NOT apply two other vendor-adjacent
   992	// gates a real dispatch enforces, and this is not an oversight:
   993	//   - assertVendorDispatchable() (adapter.dispatchDisabled, e.g. agy's headless-
   994	//     output gate): its own doc comment (dispatch.js) is explicit that
   995	//     "Non-dispatch surfaces (doctor / --vendors / --resolve) do NOT call this,
   996	//     so a disabled vendor is still listed + introspectable" — applying it here
   997	//     would make --resolve unable to preview a disabled vendor at all, which is
   998	//     the opposite of what a dry-run preview is for.
   999	//   - assertAdapterSandboxEnforceable() / the sandbox default chain generally
  1000	//     (resolveAdapterOptsForTask): these need adapterOpts (--sandbox/--model/
  1001	//     --reasoning/etc), and --resolve's CLI surface never parsed those flags
  1002	//     (only --vendor, now). Threading full adapterOpts support through
  1003	//     --resolve is a separate, larger change than this vendor-override fix —
  1004	//     out of scope here, not silently dropped.
  1005	
  1006	/**
  1007	 * HOPPER-1: emit a targeted diagnostic when a resolved vendor is not a
  1008	 * registered adapter. Adapter ids are short lowercase words (codex, kimi,
  1009	 * opencode, ...); model ids carry version digits (gpt-5.5, claude-opus-4-7) or
  1010	 * a provider namespace (deepseek/v4-flash, org/model:tag). That distinction
  1011	 * tells a "you put a model where an adapter goes" mistake apart from a typo.
  1012	 */
  1013	function reportUnregisteredVendor(vendor, task, known) {
  1014	  console.error(`Error: resolved vendor '${vendor}' is not a registered adapter.`);
  1015	  console.error(`  Registered adapters: ${known.join(', ')}`);
  1016	  const looksLikeModel = /[/:]/.test(vendor) || /\d/.test(vendor);
  1017	  if (looksLikeModel) {
  1018	    console.error(`  '${vendor}' looks like a MODEL name, not an adapter.`);
  1019	    console.error(`  The Vendor column (queue.md) and task-vendor-preference table (AGENTS.md) name`);
  1020	    console.error(`  an ADAPTER — the CLI to invoke — while the model is selected separately:`);
  1021	    console.error(`    1. put an adapter in the Vendor column for '${task.id}' (e.g. 'codex' or 'opencode'), and`);
  1022	    console.error(`    2. pass the model via the flag:  hopper-dispatch ${task.id} --model ${vendor}`);
  1023	  } else {
  1024	    console.error(`  '${vendor}' is not a known adapter — likely a typo.`);
  1025	    console.error(`  Fix the Vendor column for '${task.id}' in queue.md, or the task-vendor-preference`);
  1026	    console.error(`  table in AGENTS.md, to one of: ${known.join(', ')}.`);
  1027	  }
  1028	}
  1029	
  1030	/**
  1031	 * P3a-fix: shared soft-warn helper, called from both sync and background dispatch
  1032	 * paths. Non-blocking, advisory only — vendor may have a fresh model not yet
  1033	 * probed. Caller catches any error to ensure dispatch is never blocked.
  1034	 */
  1035	function warnIfModelUnknown(vendor, model) {
  1036	  if (!model) return;
  1037	  const cached = getVendorCache(vendor);
  1038	  // HOPPER-2: a never-probed machine (no cache file, or a cache entry whose
  1039	  // model list is empty) used to get ZERO feedback for a typo'd --model. Give a
  1040	  // clear, actionable hint instead. We deliberately do NOT auto-probe here:
  1041	  // probing spawns a vendor subprocess and the dispatch path must stay
  1042	  // single-spawn and must never write the cache (spec §3 #4 + §14.6). The user
  1043	  // opts in to populating the cache via --probe.
  1044	  if (!cached || !Array.isArray(cached.models) || cached.models.length === 0) {
  1045	    console.error(`  note: no probed model cache for ${vendor}; cannot validate --model '${model}'.`);
  1046	    console.error(`  proceeding anyway. To check model names ahead of time:`);
  1047	    console.error(`  (a) RECOMMENDED — omit --model entirely; ${vendor} has an implicit default model.`);
  1048	    console.error(`  (b) Populate the cache once: \`hopper-dispatch --probe ${vendor}\`, then re-dispatch.`);
  1049	    return;
  1050	  }
  1051	  // Form-tolerant membership: a cached `xiaomi/mimo-v2.5-pro` should satisfy a
  1052	  // normalized bare `mimo-v2.5-pro` (and vice versa), matching V3/V4 canon rules.
  1053	  const inCache = cached.models.some((m) => { try { return modelKeysMatch(vendor, m, model); } catch (_) { return m === model; } });
  1054	  if (!inCache) {
  1055	    console.error(`  warn: model '${model}' not in cached list for ${vendor}`);
  1056	    console.error(`  cached models (${staleness(cached.probed_at)}): ${cached.models.slice(0, 8).join(', ')}${cached.models.length > 8 ? '...' : ''}`);
  1057	    console.error(`  proceeding anyway; three fix paths:`);
  1058	    // Phase 6c follow-up (user question 2026-05-21 "what if I don't know
  1059	    // the model name?"): generalize the omit-model hint across ALL vendors.
  1060	    // Every vendor has an implicit default — --model is optional. This
  1061	    // makes "omit --model" the first-class recommended path for users who
  1062	    // don't remember a specific model identifier.
  1063	    console.error(`  (a) RECOMMENDED — omit --model entirely; ${vendor} has an implicit default model.`);
  1064	    console.error(`      hopper-dispatch <task-id>                              # default model + default reasoning`);
  1065	    console.error(`      hopper-dispatch <task-id> --reasoning high             # default model + elevated reasoning`);
  1066	    console.error(`  (b) Refresh the cache: \`hopper-dispatch --probe ${vendor}\` (the vendor may have added '${model}' since last probe).`);
  1067	    if (vendor === 'kimi' && ['partial', 'config-only'].includes(cached.introspection_supported)) {
  1068	      console.error(`  (c) Refresh Kimi's configured aliases with \`kimi provider list --json\`, or add one to ~/.kimi-code/config.toml (legacy ~/.kimi/ for old Python kimi-cli) then re-probe:`);
  1069	      console.error(`      [models."${model}"]`);
  1070	      console.error(`      model = "kimi-for-coding"   # or another Moonshot upstream ID`);
  1071	      console.error(`      capabilities = ["thinking"]   # if your target supports thinking`);
  1072	    } else {
  1073	      console.error(`  (c) Verify the exact identifier with \`hopper-dispatch --models ${vendor}\` (or /hopper:models ${vendor}).`);
  1074	    }
  1075	  } else if (isStale(cached.probed_at)) {
  1076	    console.error(`  note: cache for ${vendor} is ${staleness(cached.probed_at)}; consider \`hopper-dispatch --probe ${vendor}\` to refresh`);
  1077	  }
  1078	}
  1079	
  1080	// Dispatch may only consume an existing cache snapshot. Never infer observed
  1081	// runtime models from cache values and never probe or mutate cache here.
  1082	function attestationCatalogSnapshot(vendor, adapter) {
  1083	  const cached = getVendorCache(vendor);
  1084	  const provenance = cached?.catalog_provenance ?? cached?.catalogProvenance ?? {};
  1085	  return {
  1086	    sourceKind: provenance.source_kind ?? provenance.sourceKind ?? 'unknown',
  1087	    sourceLabel: provenance.source_label ?? provenance.sourceLabel ?? 'unknown',
  1088	    observedAt: provenance.observed_at ?? provenance.observedAt ?? null,
  1089	    freshness: provenance.freshness ?? 'unknown',
  1090	    binaryAvailability: provenance.binary_availability ?? provenance.binaryAvailability ?? 'unknown',
  1091	    binaryBasename: provenance.binary_basename ?? provenance.binaryBasename ?? null,
  1092	    binding: adapter?.selectorMetadataBinding ?? null,
  1093	  };
  1094	}
  1095	
  1096	function attestationStartupSnapshot(effectiveAdapterOpts, vendor, adapter) {
  1097	  return buildAttestationStartupSnapshot({
  1098	    requestedSelector: effectiveAdapterOpts.requestedSelector,
  1099	    effectiveSelector: effectiveAdapterOpts.effectiveSelector,
  1100	    effectiveSelectorSource: effectiveAdapterOpts.effectiveSelectorSource,
  1101	    catalog: attestationCatalogSnapshot(vendor, adapter),
  1102	    selectorMetadata: adapter?.selectorMetadata ? { adapterManifest: adapter.selectorMetadata } : {},
  1103	    binding: adapter?.selectorMetadataBinding ?? null,
  1104	  });
  1105	}
  1106	
  1107	async function runDispatch(hopperDir, taskId, { write = false, force = false, adapterOpts = {}, vendorOverride = null, adhocSpec = null } = {}) {
  1108	  try {
  1109	    const optsSummary = Object.keys(adapterOpts).length > 0
  1110	      ? ` [opts: ${Object.entries(adapterOpts).map(([k, v]) => `${k}=${v}`).join(', ')}]`
  1111	      : '';
  1112	    console.error(`hopper-dispatch v${VERSION} — dispatching ${taskId}${optsSummary}...`);
  1113	
  1114	    const resolved = adhocSpec
  1115	      ? await resolveAdhocDispatch({ hopperDir, taskType: adhocSpec.taskType, brief: adhocSpec.brief, id: taskId, vendorOverride })
  1116	      : await resolveDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride });
  1117	    // Operator-facing provenance of the task content (never sent to the vendor).
  1118	    if (resolved.specNotice) console.error(`  notice: ${resolved.specNotice}`);
  1119	    const effectiveAdapterOpts = resolveAdapterOptsForTask(resolved, adapterOpts);
  1120	    if (effectiveAdapterOpts.sandbox) {
  1121	      // When the resolver overrides an EXPLICIT request (only codex does this: its -s sandbox
  1122	      // is broken on Windows, so it's pinned to full-access), say so plainly instead of
  1123	      // mislabeling the resolved value as "explicit" — the operator asked for X, got full-access.
  1124	      let source;
  1125	      if (adapterOpts.sandbox && adapterOpts.sandbox !== effectiveAdapterOpts.sandbox) {
  1126	        source = `${resolved.vendor}: -s ${adapterOpts.sandbox} unsupported here → full-access`;
  1127	      } else {
  1128	        source = adapterOpts.sandbox ? 'explicit' : 'auto';
  1129	      }
  1130	      console.error(`  permission: sandbox=${effectiveAdapterOpts.sandbox} (${source})`);
  1131	    }
  1132	    // Batch 2: policy-chain notices — model-rule/effort-policy resolution + sentinel
  1133	    // resolution + effort clamp visibility. Never blocks dispatch (advisory only).
  1134	    for (const notice of effectiveAdapterOpts.policyNotices || []) {
  1135	      console.error(`  notice: ${notice}`);
  1136	    }
  1137	
  1138	    // hostVendor: an explicit HOPPER_HOST_VENDOR (set by every Tier-C wrapper) always
  1139	    // wins; when unset, resolveHostIdentity() self-detects Claude Code (Tier B has no
  1140	    // wrapper) via cli/src/host-detect.js. The comparison itself is family-based, not
  1141	    // string equality — see VENDOR_FAMILY in validation.js for why.
  1142	    const { id: hostVendor } = resolveHostIdentity();
  1143	    const hostCheck = validateHostVendorSeparation(hostVendor, resolved.vendor);
  1144	    if (hostCheck.notice) console.error(`  notice: ${hostCheck.notice}`);
  1145	    // Dispatch gate for a capability-disabled vendor (agy headless-output) is enforced at the
  1146	    // shared spawn chokepoint executeWithAdapter() below; nothing extra needed on the sync path.
  1147	
  1148	    // Soft-warn: --model passed but X not in cached list. Non-blocking. Single-
  1149	    // spawn invariant unaffected (resolveDispatch is in-process; cache read is FS).
  1150	    if (effectiveAdapterOpts.model) {
  1151	      // Warn on the NORMALIZED model (matches the --background path); otherwise a
  1152	      // user-typed `GPT-5.5` would false-warn "not in cached list" then run `gpt-5.5`.
  1153	      try { warnIfModelUnknown(resolved.vendor, effectiveAdapterOpts.model); } catch (_) { /* never block dispatch */ }
  1154	    }
  1155	
  1156	    const startedAt = Date.now();
  1157	    const adapter = getAdapter(resolved.vendor);
  1158	    if (!adapter) {
  1159	      throw new Error(`unknown vendor '${resolved.vendor}'`);
  1160	    }
  1161	    const startupSnapshot = attestationStartupSnapshot(effectiveAdapterOpts, resolved.vendor, adapter);
  1162	    const result = await executeWithAdapter({ resolved, adapter, adapterOpts: effectiveAdapterOpts, hopperDir, cwd: resolveVendorCwd(hopperDir) });
  1163	    const elapsedMs = Date.now() - startedAt;
  1164	
  1165	    console.log('');
  1166	    console.log('=== DISPATCH RESULT ===');
  1167	    console.log(`Task:     ${result.task.id} (${result.task.taskType})`);
  1168	    console.log(`Vendor:   ${result.vendor}`);
  1169	    console.log(`Status:   ${result.output.status}`);
  1170	    console.log(`Duration: ${elapsedMs}ms`);
  1171	    if (result.output.usage) {
  1172	      console.log(`Usage:    ${JSON.stringify(result.output.usage)}`);
  1173	    }
  1174	    console.log('');
  1175	    if (result.output.status === 'success') {
  1176	      console.log('--- OUTPUT ---');
  1177	      console.log(result.output.text);
  1178	      console.log('--- END OUTPUT ---');
  1179	    } else {
  1180	      console.log(`--- ${result.output.status.toUpperCase()} ---`);
  1181	      console.log(publicAdapterDiagnostic(result.output));
  1182	      console.log('--- END ---');
  1183	    }
  1184	
  1185	    if (process.env.HOPPER_DEBUG) {
  1186	      console.log('');
  1187	      console.log('=== RAW SUBPROCESS RESULT ===');
  1188	      console.log(JSON.stringify({
  1189	        exitCode: result.raw.exitCode,
  1190	        timedOut: result.raw.timedOut,
  1191	        durationMs: result.raw.durationMs,
  1192	        stdoutLen: result.raw.stdout.length,
  1193	        stderrLen: result.raw.stderr.length,
  1194	        logFileLen: result.raw.logFileContent ? result.raw.logFileContent.length : null,
  1195	      }, null, 2));
  1196	    }
  1197	
  1198	    if (write) {
  1199	      try {
  1200	        // req #3: the frontmatter `model` field must be the RESOLVED real name (V4-
  1201	        // normalized and/or sentinel-resolved), not the raw --model flag literal —
  1202	        // e.g. `--model verified-latest` must record the concrete model it resolved
  1203	        // to, not the sentinel string. effectiveAdapterOpts.model is that resolved
  1204	        // value (background dispatch already used the equivalent effectiveOpts here;
  1205	        // this was the one path still threading the pre-resolution raw flag through).
  1206	        const written = await writeOutput({ hopperDir, dispatchResult: result, force, model: effectiveAdapterOpts.model });
  1207	        // Existing sync formatting remains the body contract. Wrap it in an
  1208	        // in-progress frontmatter record, then use the same event-first
  1209	        // finalizer as hopper-runner so --write cannot skip JSONL evidence.
  1210	        const initial = readFrontmatter(written.path);
  1211	        writeFrontmatter(written.path, {
  1212	          task_id: result.task.id,
  1213	          adapter: result.vendor,
  1214	          model: publicModelIdentifier(effectiveAdapterOpts.model, result.vendor) || '(vendor default)',
  1215	          ...startupSnapshot.frontmatter,
  1216	          status: 'in-progress',
  1217	          mode: 'sync',
  1218	          start_time: new Date(startedAt).toISOString(),
  1219	          end_time: null,
  1220	          terminal_event_emitted: false,
  1221	          _body: initial._body,
  1222	        });
  1223	        const terminalStatus = result.output.status === 'success' ? 'done' : 'failed';
  1224	        finalizeTerminalAttestation({
  1225	          hopperDir,
  1226	          taskId: result.task.id,
  1227	          outputMdPath: written.path,
  1228	          startupSnapshot,
  1229	          parsed: result.output,
  1230	          completion: {
  1231	            vendor: result.vendor,
  1232	            status: terminalStatus,
  1233	            phase: terminalStatus,
  1234	            message: terminalStatus === 'done' ? 'Task completed successfully.' : 'Task failed.',
  1235	            source: 'sync',
  1236	            durationMs: result.raw.durationMs ?? elapsedMs,
  1237	            exitCode: result.raw.exitCode,
  1238	            adapterStatus: result.output.status,
  1239	            adapterDiagnosticCode: publicAdapterDiagnostic(result.output),
  1240	          },
  1241	        });
  1242	        console.log('');
  1243	        console.log('=== OUTPUT.MD WRITTEN ===');
  1244	        console.log(`Wrote: ${written.path}${written.overwritten ? ' (overwritten)' : ''}`);
  1245	        console.log('');
  1246	        console.log('--- Suggested queue.md edit ---');
  1247	        console.log(written.queueEdit);
  1248	        console.log('');
  1249	        console.log('--- Suggested COST-LOG.md row ---');
  1250	        console.log(written.costEdit);
  1251	        console.log('');
  1252	        console.log('Per spec §11 unified user-action gate: apply suggested edits only after manual review.');
  1253	      } catch (writeErr) {
  1254	        console.error(`Error writing output.md: ${writeErr.message}`);
  1255	        if (result.output.status === 'success') process.exit(2);  // distinct exit code for write failure
  1256	      }
  1257	    }
  1258	
  1259	    if (result.output.status !== 'success') process.exit(1);
  1260	  } catch (err) {
  1261	    console.error(`Error: ${err.message}`);
  1262	    process.exit(err.exitCode || 1);
  1263	  }
  1264	}
  1265	
  1266	// ─── Background mode (spec §14) ───────────────────────────────────────
  1267	
  1268	async function runBackgroundDispatch(hopperDir, taskId, { adapterOpts = {}, vendorOverride = null, adhocSpec = null, exitOnError = true } = {}) {
  1269	  // exitOnError=false (the --swarm path) throws instead of process.exit, so a failed
  1270	  // panelist does NOT abort the rest of the panel.
  1271	  const bail = (msg, code = 1) => {
  1272	    if (exitOnError) { console.error(msg); process.exit(code); }
  1273	    const e = new Error(msg.replace(/^Error:\s*/, '')); e.exitCode = code; throw e;
  1274	  };
  1275	  // We need a resolved adapter + argv WITHOUT executing. resolveDispatch
  1276	  // returns the composed prompt; we then ask the adapter for argv.
  1277	  let resolved;
  1278	  try {
  1279	    resolved = adhocSpec
  1280	      ? await resolveAdhocDispatch({ hopperDir, taskType: adhocSpec.taskType, brief: adhocSpec.brief, id: taskId, vendorOverride })
  1281	      : await resolveDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride });
  1282	  } catch (err) {
  1283	    bail(`Error: ${err.message}`, 1);
  1284	  }
  1285	  // Operator-facing provenance of the task content (never sent to the vendor).
  1286	  if (resolved.specNotice) console.error(`  notice: ${resolved.specNotice}`);
  1287	  const effectiveAdapterOpts = resolveAdapterOptsForTask(resolved, adapterOpts);
  1288	
  1289	  const adapter = getAdapter(resolved.vendor);
  1290	  if (!adapter) {
  1291	    bail(`Error: unknown vendor '${resolved.vendor}'`, 1);
  1292	  }
  1293	  try {
  1294	    // Keep the Kimi fail-closed contract ahead of optional process-guard setup.
  1295	    // Otherwise Windows/Linux report the unavailable macOS backend (exit 1) for
  1296	    // a Kimi read-only request rather than the canonical no-spawn exit 2.
  1297	    assertAdapterSandboxEnforceable(adapter, effectiveAdapterOpts);
  1298	  } catch (err) {
  1299	    bail(`Error: ${err.message}`, err.exitCode || 2);
  1300	  }
  1301	
  1302	  try {
  1303	    prepareSubjectRootGuard({ subjectRoot: effectiveAdapterOpts.subjectRoot, sandbox: effectiveAdapterOpts.sandbox });
  1304	  } catch (err) {
  1305	    bail(`Error: ${err.message}`, 1);
  1306	  }
  1307	
  1308	  // Batch 2: policy-chain notices (model-rule/effort-policy resolution, sentinel
  1309	  // resolution, effort clamp visibility) — same source as the sync path.
  1310	  for (const notice of effectiveAdapterOpts.policyNotices || []) {
  1311	    console.error(`  notice: ${notice}`);
  1312	  }
  1313	
  1314	  // P3a-fix: soft-warn also fires on --background path. Same warnIfModelUnknown
  1315	  // helper as sync dispatch; advisory only, never blocks.
  1316	  if (effectiveAdapterOpts.model) {
  1317	    try { warnIfModelUnknown(resolved.vendor, effectiveAdapterOpts.model); } catch (_) { /* never block */ }
  1318	  }
  1319	
  1320	  // Enforce host != vendor (family-based; explicit HOPPER_HOST_VENDOR > self-detection
  1321	  // for Tier B/Claude Code — see cli/src/host-detect.js + VENDOR_FAMILY in validation.js).
  1322	  const { id: hostVendor } = resolveHostIdentity();
  1323	  try {
  1324	    const hostCheck = validateHostVendorSeparation(hostVendor, resolved.vendor);
  1325	    if (hostCheck.notice) console.error(`  notice: ${hostCheck.notice}`);
  1326	  } catch (err) {
  1327	    bail(`Error: ${err.message}`, 1);
  1328	  }
  1329	
  1330	  // Dispatch gate: a vendor disabled by capability (e.g. agy headless-output unsupported) is
  1331	  // blocked here unless explicitly opted in (covers background + swarm panelists).
  1332	  try {
  1333	    assertVendorDispatchable(resolved.vendor);
  1334	  } catch (err) {
  1335	    bail(`Error: ${err.message}`, 1);
  1336	  }
  1337	
  1338	  // Adapter envPreflight gate
  1339	  const preflight = adapter.envPreflight();
  1340	  if (!preflight.ok) {
  1341	    bail(`Error: adapter ${resolved.vendor} preflight failed: ${preflight.missing.join(' | ')}`, 1);
  1342	  }
  1343	  if (preflight.missing && preflight.missing.length > 0) {
  1344	    for (const msg of preflight.missing) console.error(`Note: ${msg}`);
  1345	  }
  1346	
  1347	  // Adapter stdin handling
  1348	  if (adapter.stdinMode === 'pipe') {
  1349	    console.error('Use sync mode (omit --background) for this vendor, or update the adapter to accept prompt as argv.');
  1350	    bail(`Error: adapter ${resolved.vendor} requires stdinMode='pipe'; background mode does not support stdin piping (spec §14).`, 1);
  1351	  }
  1352	
  1353	  // Build argv. logFile gets injected by spawnDetached path NOT here — adapter
  1354	  // sees logFile via opts only if it needs it. For background, the log lives
  1355	  // at <task-id>-output.log next to the .md.
  1356	  const handoffDir = join(hopperDir, 'handoffs');
  1357	  const outputMdPath = join(handoffDir, `${taskId}-output.md`);
  1358	  const logPath = outputMdPath.replace(/\.md$/, '.log');
  1359	  // Phase 6c F1: thread taskType through so runner's timeoutMs() applies review floor.
  1360	  const effectiveOpts = { ...effectiveAdapterOpts, background: true, logFile: logPath, taskType: resolved.task.taskType, cwd: resolveVendorCwd(hopperDir) };

    36	/**
    37	 * Announce that a PLATFORM ROUTER is about to run unpinned, with the provider
    38	 * ids needed to fix it. No-op for any vendor that does not declare itself one.
    39	 *
    40	 * A platform router (pi) serves users on gpt / claude / kimi / qwen / glm…, so
    41	 * hopper must not guess a model for it — but running unannounced is how a swarm
    42	 * panelist ran on `gpt-5.5` out of ~/.pi/agent/settings.json while every other
    43	 * dispatch in that project used the 5.6 line, with `requested_selector: null`
    44	 * and nothing to show it. Warn rather than refuse (operator decision
    45	 * 2026-08-11): a refusal would break every existing unpinned dispatch, and the
    46	 * run is still attested after the fact by `observed_models`.
    47	 *
    48	 * The ids are load-bearing, not decoration: pi rejects every intuitive name
    49	 * (`kimi`, `qwen`, `gemini`, `claude`, `grok` → `provider_not_found`), so a
    50	 * caller told only "pin a model" cannot act on it.
    51	 *
    52	 * @param {string[]} notices  collected policyNotices, appended in place
    53	 * @param {string} vendor
    54	 */
    55	function pushUnpinnedPlatformNotice(notices, vendor) {
    56	  let providers;
    57	  try { providers = getAdapter(vendor)?.capabilities?.modelArg?.platformProviders; } catch (_) { return; }
    58	  if (!Array.isArray(providers) || providers.length === 0) return;
    59	  notices.push(
    60	    `NOT PINNED: '${vendor}' is a multi-provider platform and hopper does not guess a model for it — `
    61	    + `this dispatch runs on whatever ${vendor} picks itself, and only the recorded observed_models will `
    62	    + 'say which. Settle it once in .hopper/AGENTS.md `## Approved Vendors` → `Default model` '
    63	    + `(or HOPPER_${String(vendor).toUpperCase()}_MODEL), as \`<provider>/<model>\`. Provider ids: `
    64	    + `${providers.map((p) => p.id).join(', ')} — \`hopper-dispatch --capabilities ${vendor}\` maps each `
    65	    + 'to its vendor and auth method (the intuitive names are rejected).',
    66	  );
    67	}
