    69	/** Raw Effort policy / Model rule cells for a task-type, or the unbound-shaped default. */
    70	function policyForTaskType(agentsData, taskType) {
    71	  return (agentsData && agentsData.policies && agentsData.policies[taskType]) || { effortPolicy: '', modelRule: '' };
    72	}
    73	
    74	/**
    75	 * Resolve a task for dispatch (Phase 1 stops here; Phase 2 calls vendor adapter).
    76	 *
    77	 * @param {object} args
    78	 * @param {string} args.hopperDir          Path to .hopper/ directory
    79	 * @param {string} args.taskId             Task ID to dispatch
    80	 * @returns {Promise<{
    81	 *   task: import('./types.js').TaskRow,
    82	 *   frame: string,
    83	 *   vendor: string,
    84	 *   composedPrompt: string,
    85	 *   taskSpec: string,
    86	 *   specNotice: string|null
    87	 * }>}
    88	 */
    89	export async function resolveDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride = null }) {
    90	  // 1. Read queue.md, find task by ID
    91	  const queuePath = join(hopperDir, 'queue.md');
    92	  const tasks = await parseQueue(queuePath);
    93	  const { task, reason } = findEligibleTask(tasks, taskId);
    94	  if (!task) {
    95	    throw new Error(`Task not eligible: ${reason}`);
    96	  }
    97	
    98	  // 2. Load task-type frame
    99	  const frame = await loadTaskFrame(hopperDir, task.taskType);
   100	
   101	  // 3. Resolve vendor via AGENTS.md (deterministic, no retry state)
   102	  const agentsPath = join(hopperDir, 'AGENTS.md');
   103	  const agentsData = await parseAgentsFile(agentsPath);
   104	  // --vendor override wins over the AGENTS.md routing tables; host != vendor and
   105	  // unknown-vendor checks still apply downstream (the dispatcher validates both).
   106	  const vendor = vendorOverride || resolveVendor(task, agentsData);
   107	  // Project-level Approved Vendors whitelist (batch 3, TH-approved-vendors):
   108	  // enforced AFTER vendor resolution so a --vendor override is checked too,
   109	  // not just AGENTS.md-routed dispatches. Independent of, and does not
   110	  // short-circuit, the host!=vendor isomorphism guard applied downstream.
   111	  assertVendorApproved(agentsData, vendor);
   112	
   113	  // 4. Build the task CONTENT: the detailed spec section from
   114	  //    handoffs/leader-tasklist.md (when there is one) MERGED with the queue.md
   115	  //    Brief cell — never one silently standing in for the other.
   116	  //
   117	  //    WHY MERGE (and not "pick one"): the execution-mode guardrail composed into
   118	  //    every handoff states, verbatim, "The brief and Task spec below are the
   119	  //    complete, closed loop." (cli/src/tasks.js). Dropping either half would make
   120	  //    that sentence false for the vendor reading it — the very class of defect
   121	  //    this path used to have: loadTaskSpec's two miss branches returned a
   122	  //    PLACEHOLDER STRING claiming "using queue.md brief only" which then became
   123	  //    the spec, so the brief never reached the vendor at all and the vendor got a
   124	  //    frame with no task in it (and still exited 0 / status done).
   125	  const { taskSpec, specNotice } = await composeTaskContent({ hopperDir, taskId, task });
   126	
   127	  // 5. Resolve optional governance overlay (keyed on the resolved vendor) and
   128	  // compose. resolveGovernance is pure file I/O — no subprocess (spec §3 #4).
   129	  const governance = await resolveGovernance({ hopperDir, vendor, task });
   130	  const composedPrompt = composePrompt(frame, taskSpec, { governance });
   131	
   132	  // Batch 2: raw Effort policy / Model rule cells for this task-type, consumed by
   133	  // resolveAdapterOptsForTask's --reasoning / --model fallback chains below.
   134	  const policy = policyForTaskType(agentsData, task.taskType);
   135	
   136	  return { task, frame, vendor, composedPrompt, taskSpec, specNotice, policy, vendorDefaultModel: vendorDefaultModel(agentsData, vendor) };
   137	}
   138	
   139	/**
   140	 * Resolve an AD-HOC dispatch — a one-off task with NO queue.md row; the brief IS
   141	 * the spec. Used by the directed commands (/hopper:review|research|market) and the
   142	 * swarm so they need not author (and pollute) queue.md. Returns the same shape as
   143	 * resolveDispatch, so the caller's single-spawn / host!=vendor guarantees apply
   144	 * identically. Read-only/web-search task-type defaults still come from
   145	 * resolveAdapterOptsForTask downstream.
   146	 *
   147	 * @param {object} args
   148	 * @param {string} args.hopperDir
   149	 * @param {string} args.taskType        a scaffolded task-type (its frame must exist)
   150	 * @param {string} args.brief           the task brief (becomes the spec)
   151	 * @param {string} args.id              the synthetic task-id (for output files)
   152	 * @param {string|null} [args.vendorOverride]
   153	 * @returns {Promise<{task: object, frame: string, vendor: string, composedPrompt: string, taskSpec: string}>}
   154	 */
   155	export async function resolveAdhocDispatch({ hopperDir, taskType, brief, id, vendorOverride = null }) {
   156	  validateTaskId(id);
   157	  if (typeof taskType !== 'string' || !TASK_TYPE_PATTERN.test(taskType)) {
   158	    throw new Error(`Invalid --task-type "${taskType}" (expected lowercase like prd-research / code-review-acceptance).`);
   159	  }
   160	  if (typeof brief !== 'string' || brief.trim().length === 0) {
   161	    throw new Error('Ad-hoc dispatch requires a non-empty --brief.');
   162	  }
   163	  // Frame must exist for the task-type (same loader as the queued path).
   164	  const frame = await loadTaskFrame(hopperDir, taskType);
   165	  const task = { id, taskType, brief, status: 'pending', depends: [] };  // shape parity with queue TaskRow
   166	  const agentsData = await parseAgentsFile(join(hopperDir, 'AGENTS.md'));
   167	  const vendor = vendorOverride || resolveVendor(task, agentsData);
   168	  if (!vendor) {
   169	    throw new Error(`No vendor resolved for ad-hoc task-type "${taskType}". Pass --vendor <name> (no AGENTS.md preference found).`);
   170	  }
   171	  // Approved Vendors whitelist — same enforcement as resolveDispatch's queue path,
   172	  // covering the ad-hoc path (/hopper:review|research|market, swarm panelists).
   173	  assertVendorApproved(agentsData, vendor);
   174	  const taskSpec = brief;
   175	  const governance = await resolveGovernance({ hopperDir, vendor, task });
   176	  const composedPrompt = composePrompt(frame, taskSpec, { governance });
   177	  const policy = policyForTaskType(agentsData, taskType);
   178	  // specNotice: shape parity with resolveDispatch. Always null here — an ad-hoc
   179	  // dispatch has no leader-tasklist lookup to miss (the brief IS the spec, and an
   180	  // empty one was already rejected above).
   181	  return { task, frame, vendor, composedPrompt, taskSpec, specNotice: null, policy, vendorDefaultModel: vendorDefaultModel(agentsData, vendor) };
   182	}
   183	
   184	/**
   185	 * Plan a multi-vendor SWARM (panel) — fan the SAME qualitative brief out to N vendors,
   186	 * each as its own ad-hoc dispatch (one single-spawn per panelist; N tasks, not N retries).
   187	 * PURE: validates + returns the per-panelist plan; the caller dispatches each via the
   188	 * normal ad-hoc path. Restricted to READ-ONLY/qualitative task-types — swarming a write
   189	 * task would have N vendors edit the same files. The vendor *selection* + per-vendor config
   190	 * is a host-side confirmation gate; this only executes the confirmed list.
   191	 *
   192	 * @param {object} args
   193	 * @param {string} args.taskType   a read-only/qualitative task-type (review/research/audit)
   194	 * @param {string} args.brief
   195	 * @param {string[]|string} args.vendors   panelists (array or comma-separated)
   196	 * @param {string} [args.idBase]
   197	 * @returns {Array<{ vendor: string, id: string, taskType: string, brief: string }>}
   198	 */
   199	export function planSwarm({ taskType, brief, vendors, idBase, now = Date.now() }) {
   200	  if (typeof taskType !== 'string' || !TASK_TYPE_PATTERN.test(taskType)) {
   201	    throw new Error(`Invalid --task-type "${taskType}".`);
   202	  }
   203	  if (!READ_ONLY_DEFAULT_TASK_TYPES.includes(taskType)) {
   204	    throw new Error(`--swarm only supports read-only/qualitative task-types (${READ_ONLY_DEFAULT_TASK_TYPES.join(', ')}); got "${taskType}". Swarming a write task would have N vendors edit the same files.`);
   205	  }
   206	  if (typeof brief !== 'string' || brief.trim().length === 0) {
   207	    throw new Error('--swarm requires a non-empty --brief.');
   208	  }
   209	  const list = (Array.isArray(vendors) ? vendors : String(vendors || '').split(','))
   210	    .map((v) => v.trim()).filter(Boolean);
   211	  const uniq = [...new Set(list)];
   212	  if (uniq.length < 2) {
   213	    throw new Error('--swarm needs at least 2 vendors (--vendors v1,v2,...). Use --adhoc for a single vendor.');
   214	  }
   215	  for (const v of uniq) {
   216	    if (!VENDOR_PATTERN.test(v)) throw new Error(`Invalid vendor name "${v}" in --vendors.`);
   217	  }
   218	  const base = idBase || `swarm-${taskType}-${now.toString(36)}`;
   219	  validateTaskId(base);
   220	  return uniq.map((vendor) => ({ vendor, id: `${base}-${vendor}`, taskType, brief }));
   221	}
   222	
   223	/** Absolute path of the optional detailed-spec file. Single source of truth. */
   224	export function leaderTasklistPath(hopperDir) {
   225	  return join(hopperDir, 'handoffs', 'leader-tasklist.md');
   226	}
   227	
   228	/**
   229	 * Load the DETAILED spec section for a task from .hopper/handoffs/leader-tasklist.md.
   230	 *
   231	 * Returns the section text, or `null` when there is no detailed spec — either
   232	 * because the file has no section for this task-id, or because the file does not
   233	 * exist at all. It NEVER returns prose describing its own failure: this function
   234	 * used to return placeholder strings like
   235	 *   "(no detailed spec found for T-1 in leader-tasklist.md; using queue.md brief only)"
   236	 * which the caller then handed to composePrompt AS the task spec — so the vendor
   237	 * received a handoff whose entire "Task spec" section was a sentence about a
   238	 * missing file, the queue.md Brief was never composed in at all, and the sentence
   239	 * itself was false ("using queue.md brief only" while using nothing). Missing data
   240	 * is now reported as absence (`null`); the caller decides the fallback.
   241	 *
   242	 * Any non-ENOENT I/O error still throws — an unreadable/permission-denied
   243	 * leader-tasklist.md is a real fault and must not be laundered into "no spec".
   244	 *
   245	 * @param {string} hopperDir
   246	 * @param {string} taskId
   247	 * @returns {Promise<string|null>}  section text, or null when there is none
   248	 */
   249	export async function loadTaskSpec(hopperDir, taskId) {
   250	  const path = leaderTasklistPath(hopperDir);
   251	  let content;
   252	  try {
   253	    content = await readFile(path, 'utf-8');
   254	  } catch (err) {
   255	    if (err.code === 'ENOENT') return null;
   256	    throw err;
   257	  }
   258	  // Find a section starting with **<task-id>** or ## <task-id> or ### <task-id>
   259	  const escapedId = taskId.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
   260	  const sectionStart = content.search(new RegExp(
   261	    `(\\*\\*${escapedId}\\*\\*|^##+\\s+${escapedId}\\b|^\\|\\s*${escapedId}\\s*\\|)`,
   262	    'm'
   263	  ));
   264	  if (sectionStart === -1) return null;
   265	  // Take the next ~80 lines as the task spec (or until next H2 heading)
   266	  const rest = content.slice(sectionStart);
   267	  const nextH2 = rest.slice(50).search(/^##\s+/m);
   268	  const end = nextH2 === -1 ? Math.min(rest.length, 8000) : 50 + nextH2;
   269	  const section = rest.slice(0, end).trim();
   270	  // A section that exists but is whitespace-only is no spec either.
   271	  return section.length > 0 ? section : null;
   272	}
   273	
   274	/** Header that labels the queue.md Brief inside a merged task spec. */
   275	export const QUEUE_BRIEF_HEADING = '### Queue brief';
   276	
   277	/**
   278	 * Precedence line appended to a MERGED spec. The vendor sees two sources of task
   279	 * content; it must be told which wins, or a stale Brief cell can silently
   280	 * out-argue the detailed spec it was supposed to summarize.
   281	 */
   282	export const QUEUE_BRIEF_PRECEDENCE_NOTE =
   283	  '(Source: the Brief column of .hopper/queue.md. Where it conflicts with the detailed spec above, the detailed spec wins.)';
   284	
   285	/**
   286	 * Assemble the task CONTENT for a queued dispatch out of its two possible
   287	 * sources, and say (to the operator, never to the vendor) when one is missing.
   288	 *
   289	 * Rules:
   290	 *   - both present  → detailed spec first, then the Brief under `### Queue brief`,
   291	 *                     with an explicit "detailed spec wins on conflict" line
   292	 *   - spec only     → the spec verbatim
   293	 *   - brief only    → the brief verbatim (mirrors the ad-hoc path, where the brief
   294	 *                     IS the spec) + an operator notice explaining why there is no
   295	 *                     detailed section
   296	 *   - neither       → THROW (fail closed). Dispatching a content-free prompt is how
   297	 *                     a vendor "succeeds" (exit 0, status done) at nothing. Matches
   298	 *                     the ad-hoc/swarm paths, which already reject an empty brief.
   299	 *
   300	 * Note `??` is unusable here: queue.js gives a missing Brief cell the EMPTY STRING,
   301	 * and `'' ?? brief` is `''` — nullish coalescing only catches null/undefined.
   302	 * Everything below is emptiness-aware via .trim().
   303	 *
   304	 * @param {{hopperDir: string, taskId: string, task: {brief?: string}}} args
   305	 * @returns {Promise<{taskSpec: string, specNotice: string|null}>}
   306	 */
   307	async function composeTaskContent({ hopperDir, taskId, task }) {
   308	  const detailed = (await loadTaskSpec(hopperDir, taskId) || '').trim();
   309	  const brief = typeof task?.brief === 'string' ? task.brief.trim() : '';
   310	
   311	  if (detailed && brief) {
   312	    return {
   313	      taskSpec: `${detailed}\n\n${QUEUE_BRIEF_HEADING}\n\n${brief}\n\n${QUEUE_BRIEF_PRECEDENCE_NOTE}`,
   314	      specNotice: null,
   315	    };
   316	  }
   317	  if (detailed) return { taskSpec: detailed, specNotice: null };
   318	
   319	  // No detailed section. Distinguish "file has no entry for this id" from "no file
   320	  // at all" — same two situations the old placeholder strings described, except the
   321	  // text now goes to the OPERATOR (stderr / --resolve echo) instead of being fed to
   322	  // the vendor as if it were the task.
   323	  const path = leaderTasklistPath(hopperDir);
   324	  const tasklistExists = await fileExists(path);
   325	  if (brief) {
   326	    return {
   327	      taskSpec: brief,
   328	      specNotice: tasklistExists
   329	        ? `No detailed spec section for ${taskId} in leader-tasklist.md; task content comes from queue.md Brief.`
   330	        : `leader-tasklist.md is absent at ${path}; task content comes from queue.md Brief.`,
   331	    };
   332	  }
   333	  throw new Error(
   334	    `Task ${taskId} has no task content: queue.md Brief is empty and no detailed spec was found`
   335	    + (tasklistExists ? ` (no section for ${taskId} in ${path})` : ` (${path} does not exist)`)
   336	    + `. Fill the Brief cell for ${taskId} in .hopper/queue.md, or add a "## ${taskId}" section to leader-tasklist.md.`
   337	  );
   338	}
   339	
   340	async function fileExists(path) {
   341	  try {
   342	    await access(path);
   343	    return true;
   344	  } catch (_) {
   345	    return false;
   346	  }
   347	}
   348	
   349	/**
   350	 * Status summary for --status command.
   351	 *
   352	 * @param {string} hopperDir
   353	 */
   354	export async function getStatus(hopperDir) {
   355	  const queuePath = join(hopperDir, 'queue.md');
   356	  const tasks = await parseQueue(queuePath);
   357	  return summarizeQueue(tasks);
   358	}
   359	
   360	/**
   361	 * Return true only when the queue brief or detailed task spec explicitly says
   362	 * the task is read-only. We intentionally do not infer read-only from task-type
   363	 * names like "review"; the product default is full vendor write access unless
   364	 * the task description itself says read-only / 只读.
   365	 *
   366	 * @param {object} resolved
   367	 */
   368	export function taskTextRequestsReadOnly(resolved) {
   369	  const text = [
   370	    resolved?.task?.brief,
   370	    resolved?.task?.brief,
   371	    resolved?.taskSpec,
   372	  ].filter(Boolean).join('\n');
   373	  if (!text) return false;
   374	  if (NEGATED_READ_ONLY_RE.test(text)) return false;
   375	  return READ_ONLY_TASK_RE.test(text);
   376	}
   377	
   378	/**
   379	 * Apply the product-level default permission policy to adapter opts.
   380	 * Explicit --sandbox always wins; otherwise read-only text downgrades, and all
   381	 * other tasks default to danger-full-access.
   382	 *
   383	 * @param {object} resolved
   384	 * @param {import('./types.js').AdapterOpts} [adapterOpts]
   385	 * @returns {import('./types.js').AdapterOpts}
   386	 */
   387	export function resolveAdapterOptsForTask(resolved, adapterOpts = {}) {
   388	  const out = { ...adapterOpts };
   389	  const taskType = resolved?.task?.taskType;
   390	  // Preserve argv provenance before policy/sentinel resolution and advisory
   391	  // selector normalization mutate out.model. Runtime attestation must compare
   392	  // only the effective selector, never this requested audit value.
   393	  const requestedSelector = Object.hasOwn(adapterOpts, 'requestedSelector')
   394	    ? (typeof adapterOpts.requestedSelector === 'string' ? adapterOpts.requestedSelector : null)
   395	    : (typeof adapterOpts.model === 'string' ? adapterOpts.model : null);
   396	  const carriesEffectiveSelector = Object.hasOwn(adapterOpts, 'effectiveSelector');
   397	  const inheritedEffectiveSelector = carriesEffectiveSelector && typeof adapterOpts.effectiveSelector === 'string'
   398	    ? adapterOpts.effectiveSelector
   399	    : null;
   400	  const inheritedEffectiveSource = adapterOpts.effectiveSelectorSource;
   401	  let modelResolvedByPolicy = false;
   402	  // Batch 2: notices surfaced by the fallback chains below (policy-cell resolution,
   403	  // sentinel resolution, effort clamp visibility) — collected here and read by the
   404	  // CLI print layer as `effectiveAdapterOpts.policyNotices` immediately after this
   405	  // call. Deliberately attached NON-ENUMERABLE so it is invisible to JSON.stringify
   406	  // (background.js forwards adapterOpts to the runner via an env-JSON blob) and to
   407	  // any `{ ...effectiveAdapterOpts, ... }` spread (background/sync build effectiveOpts
   408	  // that way) — this is print-time metadata, not a real adapter option.
   409	  const notices = [];
   410	  Object.defineProperty(out, 'policyNotices', { value: notices, enumerable: false, configurable: true });
   411	
   412	  // ── --model fallback chain (batch 2): flag > AGENTS.md Model rule cell > vendor CLI default ──
   413	  // V4 normalizes a user-specified model to the vendor's canonical name (fuzzy-match
   414	  // against knownGood; advisory — passthrough if no confident match). Single
   415	  // chokepoint — every dispatch path (sync / background / adhoc / swarm) flows
   416	  // through resolveAdapterOptsForTask.
   417	  if (!out.model && !carriesEffectiveSelector && resolved?.policy?.modelRule) {
   418	    const parsedRule = parseModelRuleCell(resolved.policy.modelRule);
   419	    if (parsedRule.status === 'ok') {
   420	      out.model = parsedRule.sentinel; // resolved to a real name below (sentinel-or-normalize)
   421	      modelResolvedByPolicy = true;
   422	      notices.push(`model resolved from AGENTS.md Model rule (task-type '${taskType}'): ${parsedRule.sentinel}`);
   423	    } else if (parsedRule.status === 'unparseable') {
   424	      notices.push(`Model rule cell for task-type '${taskType}' references an unrecognized sentinel ('${String(resolved.policy.modelRule).trim()}') — ignoring; vendor CLI default will be used.`);
   425	    }
   426	    // status 'unbound' (empty / OOB `(bind per project)`) is silent — same convention
   427	    // as an unbound Default-vendor cell; falls through to the vendor's own default.
   428	  }
   429	  // ── per-vendor default (2026-08-11) ──
   430	  // Reached only when nothing more specific pinned a model. Two more levels
   431	  // before giving up and letting the vendor choose:
   432	  //
   433	  //   1. the project's `Default model` cell in AGENTS.md `## Approved Vendors`
   434	  //      — a per-vendor literal. This is the level that closes the staleness
   435	  //      gap: when a vendor ships a newer model than hopper's shipped preset,
   436	  //      a project pins it here instead of waiting for a hopper release.
   437	  //   2. the adapter's declared `hopperDefault` — hopper's own preference for
   438	  //      hopper-shaped work, which need NOT equal the vendor agent's default
   439	  //      (review/judgment tasks can justify a stronger model than the vendor
   440	  //      picks for interactive use).
   441	  //
   442	  // Without these, an unpinned dispatch silently inherited whatever the vendor
   443	  // CLI felt like: a `--swarm` panelist ran pi on gpt-5.5 out of
   444	  // ~/.pi/agent/settings.json while every other pi dispatch used the 5.6 line,
   445	  // and the handoff recorded `requested_selector: null` so nothing said so.
   446	  if (!out.model && !carriesEffectiveSelector && resolved?.vendor) {
   447	    // Machine-level override, above the project file because it describes THIS
   448	    // machine (a provider that is not logged in here, a model this account
   449	    // cannot reach) — but below an explicit --model or task-type Model rule,
   450	    // which are per-dispatch intent. Same naming as HOPPER_PI_THINKING /
   451	    // HOPPER_GROK_EFFORT.
   452	    const envKey = `HOPPER_${String(resolved.vendor).toUpperCase()}_MODEL`;
   453	    const envDefault = typeof process.env[envKey] === 'string' && process.env[envKey].trim()
   454	      ? process.env[envKey].trim()
   455	      : null;
   456	    const projectDefault = envDefault || (typeof resolved.vendorDefaultModel === 'string' && resolved.vendorDefaultModel.trim()
   457	      ? resolved.vendorDefaultModel.trim()
   458	      : null);
   459	    if (envDefault) {
   460	      out.model = envDefault;
   461	      modelResolvedByPolicy = true;
   462	      notices.push(`model resolved from ${envKey}: ${envDefault}`);
   463	    } else if (projectDefault) {
   464	      out.model = projectDefault;
   465	      modelResolvedByPolicy = true;
   466	      notices.push(`model resolved from AGENTS.md Approved Vendors 'Default model' for '${resolved.vendor}': ${projectDefault}`);
   467	    } else {
   468	      try {
   469	        const declared = resolveVerifiedLatest(getAdapter(resolved.vendor)?.capabilities?.modelArg);
   470	        if (declared) {
   471	          out.model = declared;
   472	          modelResolvedByPolicy = true;
   473	          notices.push(`model resolved from the '${resolved.vendor}' adapter's hopper default: ${declared} (override per project in AGENTS.md 'Approved Vendors' → 'Default model')`);
   474	        }
   475	        if (!declared) pushUnpinnedPlatformNotice(notices, resolved.vendor);
   476	      } catch (_) { /* unknown vendor is handled by the dispatcher; leave unpinned */ }
   477	    }
   478	  }
   479	  if (out.model && resolved?.vendor) {
   480	    try {
   481	      const modelArg = getAdapter(resolved.vendor)?.capabilities?.modelArg || {};
   482	      const kg = modelArg.knownGood || [];
   483	      if (MODEL_SENTINELS.includes(out.model)) {
   484	        // `verified-latest` (the only sentinel today) resolves to the adapter's
   485	        // DECLARED `hopperDefault` — see resolveVerifiedLatest in cli/src/policy.js
   486	        // for why that replaced the old knownGood[0] inference. The RESOLVED REAL
   487	        // NAME (not the sentinel literal) is what reaches argv + output.md
   488	        // frontmatter, because out.model is overwritten here, upstream of every
   489	        // consumer of this opts object.
   490	        const resolvedName = resolveVerifiedLatest(modelArg);
   491	        if (resolvedName) {
   492	          notices.push(`model sentinel '${out.model}' → ${resolvedName} (${resolved.vendor} hopper default)`);
   493	          out.model = resolvedName;
   494	        } else {
   495	          notices.push(`model sentinel '${out.model}': vendor '${resolved.vendor}' declares no hopper default — omitting --model so the vendor/account picks.`);
   496	          out.model = undefined;
   497	          // The sentinel path ALSO ends unpinned, and it is the path most
   498	          // dispatches actually take: the scaffold writes `Model rule:
   499	          // verified-latest` for every review task-type, which sets out.model
   500	          // above and therefore skips the per-vendor block entirely. Without
   501	          // this call the platform warning would almost never fire — verified
   502	          // live, the first version of it never printed once.
   503	          pushUnpinnedPlatformNotice(notices, resolved.vendor);
   504	        }
   505	      } else {
   506	        out.model = normalizeModel(resolved.vendor, out.model, kg);
   507	      }
   508	    } catch (_) { /* normalization is advisory; keep the original on any error */ }
   509	  }
   510	  out.requestedSelector = requestedSelector;
   511	  out.effectiveSelector = carriesEffectiveSelector ? inheritedEffectiveSelector : (out.model || null);
   512	  out.effectiveSelectorSource = ['user-argv', 'policy', 'vendor-default'].includes(inheritedEffectiveSource)
   513	    ? inheritedEffectiveSource
   514	    : (out.effectiveSelector === null
   515	      ? 'vendor-default'
   516	      : (modelResolvedByPolicy ? 'policy' : 'user-argv'));
   517	  // Permission default (precedence, most specific first):
   518	  //   1. explicit --sandbox (already in out.sandbox) wins
   519	  //   2. read-only task TEXT (brief/spec says read-only / 只读)
   520	  //   3. read-only-by-default TASK-TYPE (review / research — must not edit the repo)
   521	  //   4. global HOPPER_DEFAULT_SANDBOX, else the product default (danger-full-access)
   522	  // codex's sandbox-BYPASS is platform-split (2026-07-31; see codexSandboxBypassActive()
   523	  // in vendors/codex.js): on Windows the `-s` harness cannot spawn ANY child (1326), so
   524	  // bypass stays the default there and codex ALWAYS runs full-access regardless of the
   525	  // requested sandbox — force the resolved sandbox to full-access so the displayed value
   526	  // matches what the adapter actually runs (this overrides even an explicit --sandbox;
   527	  // showing read-only while actually running full-access would be a lie). On macOS/Linux
   528	  // codex's own `-s <mode>` sandbox is verified working, so bypass is OFF by default there
   529	  // and the normal precedence below applies (a read-only-default task-type genuinely gets
   530	  // `-s read-only`). `adapterOpts.platform` is a test-only override (real dispatch always
   531	  // reads the host's actual process.platform); production callers never set it.
   532	  const platformForCodex = adapterOpts.platform ?? process.platform;
   533	  const codexAlwaysFullAccess = resolved?.vendor === 'codex'
   534	    && codexSandboxBypassActive(platformForCodex);
   535	  if (codexAlwaysFullAccess) {
   536	    out.sandbox = 'danger-full-access';
   537	  } else if (!out.sandbox) {
   538	    if (taskTextRequestsReadOnly(resolved)) out.sandbox = 'read-only';
   539	    else if (taskType && READ_ONLY_DEFAULT_TASK_TYPES.includes(taskType)) out.sandbox = 'read-only';
   540	    else out.sandbox = resolveDefaultSandbox();
   541	  }
   542	  // Web search: auto-enable for web-needing task-types (prd-research / market-research)
   543	  // unless the caller already decided. An explicit --web-search sets out.webSearch=true
   544	  // before this runs; only web-capable adapters (codex/claude/copilot) act on it.
   545	  // HOPPER_WEB_SEARCH=0 opts out of the auto-enable (not out of an explicit
   546	  // --web-search, which is set above and is a decision rather than a default).
   547	  // A research task over a purely local corpus does not want live web search
   548	  // pulling external content in.
   549	  if (out.webSearch == null && process.env.HOPPER_WEB_SEARCH !== '0'
   550	      && taskType && WEB_SEARCH_TASK_TYPES.includes(taskType)) {
   551	    out.webSearch = true;
   552	  }
   553	  // ── --reasoning fallback chain (batch 2): flag > AGENTS.md Effort policy cell >
   554	  // HOPPER_DEFAULT_REASONING > xhigh. Record the source as part of the effective
   555	  // adapter contract: OpenCode can forward an operator's explicit CLI choice as
   556	  // its provider-specific --variant, while safely omitting a synthesized global
   557	  // default for arbitrary/custom providers. The source survives a second resolver
   558	  // pass in executeWithAdapter() and the background runner's JSON handoff.
   559	  // This is safe BY DESIGN together with the idle-timeout
   560	  // primitive: a slower max-effort run is not killed for being slow, only for going
   561	  // silent. Injected at the DISPATCH layer (not in each adapter), so adapters' own
   562	  // opt-in defaults — and their unit tests — are unaffected.
   563	  const inheritedReasoningSource = ['user-argv', 'policy', 'default'].includes(adapterOpts.reasoningSource)
   564	    ? adapterOpts.reasoningSource
   565	    : null;
   566	  let reasoningSource = inheritedReasoningSource
   567	    || (out.reasoning != null ? 'user-argv' : null);
   568	  if (out.reasoning == null) {
   569	    let fromPolicy = null;
   570	    if (resolved?.policy?.effortPolicy) {
   571	      const parsedEffort = parseEffortPolicyCell(resolved.policy.effortPolicy, resolved?.vendor);
   572	      if (parsedEffort.status === 'ok') {
   573	        fromPolicy = parsedEffort.value;
   574	        notices.push(`effort resolved from AGENTS.md Effort policy (task-type '${taskType}'): ${fromPolicy}`);
   575	      } else if (parsedEffort.status === 'unparseable') {
   576	        notices.push(`Effort policy cell for task-type '${taskType}' is unparseable ('${String(resolved.policy.effortPolicy).trim()}') — falling back to HOPPER_DEFAULT_REASONING/xhigh.`);
   577	      }
   578	      // 'unbound' (empty / OOB / table doesn't name this vendor) is silent — falls
   579	      // through to the next level, same convention as an unbound Default-vendor cell.
   580	    }
   581	    out.reasoning = fromPolicy || resolveDefaultReasoning();
   582	    reasoningSource = fromPolicy ? 'policy' : 'default';
   583	  }
   584	  out.reasoningSource = reasoningSource;
   585	  // Clamp visibility (req #2): a vendor that cannot accept the resolved level
   586	  // (whichever chain step it came from — flag, policy, or default) used to remap it
   587	  // SILENTLY inside the adapter (grok/copilot: xhigh->high, minimal->low). Surface
   588	  // that as an explicit notice instead. computeEffortClamp is a no-op (null notice)
   589	  // for vendors that don't clamp at all (in-range, or reasoningArg.knownGood is empty —
   590	  // Kimi/Claude/Agy and OpenCode, which has no universal provider enum).
   591	  if (out.reasoning && resolved?.vendor) {
   592	    try {
   593	      const reasoningKg = getAdapter(resolved.vendor)?.capabilities?.reasoningArg?.knownGood || [];
   594	      const clamp = computeEffortClamp(resolved.vendor, out.reasoning, reasoningKg);
   595	      if (clamp.notice) notices.push(clamp.notice);
   596	    } catch (_) { /* clamp visibility is advisory; never block dispatch */ }
   597	  }
   598	  return out;
   599	}
   600	
   601	/**
   602	 * Dispatch gate: refuse to dispatch to a vendor whose adapter declares `dispatchDisabled`
   603	 * unless the caller has explicitly opted in via that adapter's `enableEnv` (=== '1'). This is
   604	 * the single chokepoint enforced by BOTH the sync and background dispatch paths (and swarm,
   605	 * which fans out through the background path), so a disabled vendor cannot be reached by any
   606	 * route — `--vendor`, AGENTS.md routing, adhoc, or panel. Non-dispatch surfaces (doctor /
   607	 * --vendors / --resolve) do NOT call this, so a disabled vendor is still listed + introspectable.
   608	 * Throws a clear, actionable Error when blocked; returns silently otherwise.
   609	 * @param {string} vendor
   610	 * @param {Record<string,string|undefined>} [env]
   611	 */
   612	export function assertVendorDispatchable(vendor, env = process.env) {
   613	  let adapter;
   614	  try { adapter = getAdapter(vendor); } catch (_) { return; } // unknown vendor handled elsewhere
   615	  const gate = adapter && adapter.dispatchDisabled;
   616	  if (!gate) return;
   617	  if (env[gate.enableEnv] === '1') return; // explicit opt-in
   618	  throw new Error(
   619	    `Dispatch to vendor '${vendor}' is DISABLED: ${gate.reason} `
   620	    + `If you understand the limitation and still want to dispatch, set ${gate.enableEnv}=1.`,
   621	  );
   622	}
   623	
   624	/**
   625	 * Refuse an effective read-only dispatch when the selected adapter explicitly
   626	 * declares that it cannot enforce that sandbox. This is the shared sync and
   627	 * background gate; callers must pass opts after resolveAdapterOptsForTask().
   628	 */
   629	export function assertAdapterSandboxEnforceable(adapter, effectiveAdapterOpts) {
   630	  const permissions = adapter?.capabilities?.features?.permissions;
   631	  const readOnlySandbox = permissions?.readOnlySandbox;
   632	  const sandbox = effectiveAdapterOpts?.sandbox;
   633	  if (adapter?.name === 'kimi' && sandbox === 'read-only' && readOnlySandbox?.enforceable === false) {
   634	    const failureCode = readOnlySandbox.failureCode;
   635	    const error = new Error(
   636	      `${failureCode}: Kimi prompt mode has no permission or sandbox flag that can enforce read-only. `
   637	      + '`--write` is a Hopper-only output-artifact option, not a Kimi launch or permission flag; it does not change vendor permissions or make read-only enforceable. '
   638	      + 'An explicit non-read-only sandbox would instead run with unverified Kimi prompt-mode permissions and is incompatible with a read-only lane. '
   639	      + 'Use an enforceable read-only vendor, or only a Hopper-supported proven external process guard. Kimi is rejected before any vendor process or external guard can run.',
   640	    );
   641	    error.code = failureCode;
   642	    error.exitCode = 2;
   643	    throw error;
   644	  }
   645	
   646	  // Generic, DECLARATION-DRIVEN gate (added 2026-08-10 alongside the pi adapter).
   647	  // The Kimi branch above is name-keyed and stays byte-identical because its
   648	  // guidance is bespoke prose; everything else is driven by what the adapter
   649	  // declares, so a new vendor that cannot express one of hopper's sandbox modes
   650	  // is refused without another edit here.
   651	  //
   652	  // Why refuse rather than approximate: hopper's modes are ordered
   653	  // read-only < workspace-write < danger-full-access. When an adapter cannot
   654	  // express the requested mode, the only two options are to grant LESS than
   655	  // asked (the task cannot do its job) or MORE (the caller believes they are
   656	  // confined and are not). pi is the live case — it has no per-path permission
   657	  // model, so `workspace-write` would silently become unrestricted host access.
   658	  // Refusing makes the operator name the access they actually want.
   659	  const declaration = permissions?.[SANDBOX_DECLARATION_KEY[sandbox]];
   660	  if (!declaration || declaration.enforceable !== false) return;
   661	  const failureCode = declaration.failureCode || 'E_SANDBOX_UNENFORCEABLE';
   662	  const error = new Error(
   663	    `${failureCode}: vendor '${adapter?.name}' cannot enforce the requested \`${sandbox}\` sandbox. `
   664	    + `${declaration.mechanism || ''} `
   665	    + 'Refused before spawn: a sandbox hopper cannot enforce must not be presented as one that it can.',
   666	  );
   667	  error.code = failureCode;
   668	  error.exitCode = 2;
   669	  throw error;
   670	}
   671	
   672	/** hopper sandbox mode → the adapter capability key that declares its enforceability. */
   673	const SANDBOX_DECLARATION_KEY = Object.freeze({
   674	  'read-only': 'readOnlySandbox',
   675	  'workspace-write': 'workspaceWriteSandbox',
   676	  'danger-full-access': 'dangerFullAccessSandbox',
   677	});
   678	
   679	/**
   680	 * Execute dispatch end-to-end: resolve + adapter preflight + subprocess spawn + parse.
   681	 *
   682	 * Per spec §3 #4 (no harness reaction core): ONE adapter call = ONE subprocess
   683	 * spawn attempt. No retry on failure. If adapter.envPreflight() returns ok=false,
   684	 * we abort BEFORE spawning (no point invoking known-broken environment).
   685	 *
   686	 * @param {object} args
   687	 * @param {string} args.hopperDir
   688	 * @param {string} args.taskId
   689	 * @param {import('./types.js').AdapterOpts} [args.adapterOpts]
   690	 * @returns {Promise<{
   691	 *   task: import('./types.js').TaskRow,
   692	 *   vendor: string,
   693	 *   output: import('./types.js').TaskOutput,
   694	 *   raw: import('./types.js').SubprocessResult,
   695	 * }>}
   696	 */
   697	export async function executeDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId, adapterOpts = {} }) {
   698	  const resolved = await resolveDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId });
   699	  const adapter = getAdapter(resolved.vendor);
   700	  // Retro #3 fix: sync-mode vendor runs in the repo root that owns .hopper/
   701	  // (or $HOPPER_VENDOR_CWD if set), not the dir hopper-dispatch was invoked from.
   702	  return executeWithAdapter({ resolved, adapter, adapterOpts, cwd: resolveVendorCwd(hopperDir), hopperDir });
   703	}
   704	
   705	/**
   706	 * Lower-level dispatch entry: takes already-resolved task + adapter directly.
   707	 * Enables E2E testing per codex Phase 2 audit F3 (inject a fake adapter +
   708	 * counter-binary to prove one-spawn-per-dispatch end-to-end).
   709	 *
   710	 * @param {object} args
   711	 * @param {object} args.resolved      Output of resolveDispatch
   712	 * @param {import('./types.js').VendorAdapter} args.adapter
   713	 * @param {import('./types.js').AdapterOpts} [args.adapterOpts]
   714	 */
   715	export async function executeWithAdapter({ resolved, adapter, adapterOpts = {}, cwd = null, hopperDir = null }) {
   716	  const { task, vendor, composedPrompt } = resolved;
   717	  // Dispatch gate — the canonical sync spawn chokepoint (covers the CLI sync path AND any other
   718	  // caller, e.g. executeDispatch). A vendor disabled by capability (agy headless-output) is
   719	  // blocked here unless explicitly opted in. Throws before any subprocess is spawned.
   720	  assertVendorDispatchable(vendor);
   721	  const dispatchAdapterOpts = resolveAdapterOptsForTask(resolved, adapterOpts);
   722	  assertAdapterSandboxEnforceable(adapter, dispatchAdapterOpts);
   723	  // An explicit subject root is a forced process guard. Validate it before
   724	  // adapter preparation and before any vendor spawn; unsupported macOS backend
   725	  // or a non-read-only effective sandbox fail closed.
   726	  prepareSubjectRootGuard({
   727	    subjectRoot: dispatchAdapterOpts.subjectRoot,
   728	    sandbox: dispatchAdapterOpts.sandbox,
   729	  });
   730	
   731	  // envPreflight — if not ok, fail FAST without spawning subprocess
   732	  const preflight = adapter.envPreflight();
   733	  if (!preflight.ok) {
   734	    return {
   735	      task,
   736	      vendor,
   737	      output: {
   738	        text: '',
   739	        status: 'auth-fail',
   740	        error: `Adapter ${vendor} preflight failed: ${preflight.missing.join(' | ')}`,
   741	      },
   742	      raw: { exitCode: -1, stdout: '', stderr: '', timedOut: false, durationMs: 0 },
   743	    };
   744	  }
   745	
   746	  // Prepare log file if adapter wants one (codex F2 silent-fail detection)
   747	  let logPath = null;
   748	  if (typeof adapter.prepareLog === 'function') {
   749	    const hint = adapter.prepareLog(task.id, adapter.name);
   750	    logPath = hint.logPath || null;
   751	  }
   752	
   753	  // Build args (adapter may want logFile threaded through opts).
   754	  // Phase 6c F1: include task.taskType so timeoutMs can apply review-task floor.
   755	  // Thread the resolved vendor CWD through opts so adapters that take a
   756	  // working-dir flag (e.g. opencode --dir) can pass it explicitly.
   757	  const effectiveOpts = { ...dispatchAdapterOpts, logFile: logPath, taskType: task.taskType, cwd: cwd || undefined };
   758	
   759	  // Spawn subprocess ONCE (per spec §3 #4).
   760	  // Phase 6c F2: resolve adapter.command with deterministic known-install
   761	  // paths (NOT vendor-retry orchestration) so installers that don't add
   762	  // their bin to PATH (agy on Windows) still work. Resolved FIRST so size-gated
   763	  // prompt delivery knows the OS command-line regime (cmd.exe shim vs native .exe).
   764	  const resolvedCmd = resolveCommandWithKnownPaths(adapter.command, adapter.knownInstallPaths || []);
   765	  const spawnCommand = resolvedCmd ? resolvedCmd.command : adapter.command;
   766	  const prependArgs = resolvedCmd ? resolvedCmd.prependArgs : [];
   767	
   768	  // Size-gated prompt delivery (ISSUE-codex-bypass-flag-missing-from-argv): inline
   769	  // small prompts; for an over-budget command line write handoffs/<task>-prompt.md
   770	  // and pass the vendor a small "read this file" pointer. Needs hopperDir to locate
   771	  // handoffs/; without it (e.g. direct-injected E2E adapters) fall back to inline.
   772	  let args;
   773	  let delivery = null;
   774	  if (hopperDir) {
   775	    delivery = resolvePromptDelivery({
   776	      adapter, composedPrompt, opts: effectiveOpts,
   777	      resolvedCmd: spawnCommand, prependArgs,
   778	      handoffsDir: join(hopperDir, 'handoffs'), taskId: task.id,
   779	    });
   780	    if (delivery.fallbackReason) {
   500	    }
   501	    for (const r of res.refused) {
   502	      console.log(`REFUSED ${r.id} — ${r.error}`);
   503	      console.log('  (refused rather than guessed — when the structure has been hand-edited, guessing does damage. Handle it manually; see MIGRATION.md.)');
   504	      console.log('');
   505	    }
   506	    if (apply) {
   507	      for (const b of res.backups) console.log(`backup: ${b}`);
   508	      console.log(`Recorded in ${join(wsDir, '.migrations', 'log.md')}`);
   509	      console.log('');
   510	      console.log('Review before committing: migrations are additive only, but the cells they leave blank — Approved Vendors above all — are yours to decide.');
   511	    } else {
   512	      console.log('Dry run — nothing was written. To apply: hopper-dispatch --migrate-config --yes');
   513	    }
   514	    return;
   515	  }
   516	
   517	  const hopperDir = findHopperDir();
   518	  if (!hopperDir) {
   519	    if (args.includes('--watch-events') && !process.env.HOPPER_DIR) {
   520	      return;
   521	    }
   522	    console.error('Error: no .hopper/ directory found in cwd or ancestors.');
   523	    console.error('Set HOPPER_DIR env var OR run from a directory containing .hopper/.');
   524	    process.exit(1);
   525	  }
   526	
   527	  // Ad-hoc dispatch: a one-off task with NO queue.md row (the --brief IS the spec).
   528	  // Powers the directed commands (/hopper:review|research|market) + the swarm so they
   529	  // never have to author queue.md. Single-spawn / host!=vendor guarantees unchanged.
   530	  if (args.includes('--adhoc')) {
   531	    const valueAfter = (flag) => {
   532	      const i = args.indexOf(flag);
   533	      return i !== -1 && args[i + 1] !== undefined && !args[i + 1].startsWith('-') ? args[i + 1] : null;
   534	    };
   535	    const taskType = valueAfter('--task-type');
   536	    const brief = valueAfter('--brief');
   537	    if (!taskType || !brief) {
   538	      console.error('Error: --adhoc requires --task-type <type> and --brief "<text>".');
   539	      console.error('Example: hopper-dispatch --adhoc --task-type prd-research --brief "research X" --vendor codex --background');
   540	      process.exit(2);
   541	    }
   542	    const vendor = valueAfter('--vendor');
   543	    const adapterOpts = {};
   544	    try {
   545	      if (vendor !== null) {
   546	        validateVendor(vendor);
   547	        if (!listAdapters().includes(vendor)) throw new Error(`unknown vendor '${vendor}'. Registered: ${listAdapters().join(', ')}`);
   548	      }
   549	      if (args.includes('--web-search')) adapterOpts.webSearch = true;
   550	      const model = valueAfter('--model'); if (model) { validateModelName(model); adapterOpts.model = model; }
   551	      const reasoning = valueAfter('--reasoning'); if (reasoning) { validateReasoning(reasoning); adapterOpts.reasoning = reasoning; }
   552	      const sandbox = valueAfter('--sandbox'); if (sandbox) { validateSandbox(sandbox); adapterOpts.sandbox = sandbox; }
   553	      const timeout = valueAfter('--timeout'); if (timeout) { adapterOpts.timeoutOverrideMs = validateTimeout(timeout); }
   554	    } catch (err) {
   555	      console.error(`Error: ${err.message}`);
   556	      process.exit(2);
   557	    }
   558	    // Synthetic id for output files (caller may pin one via --id). Date.now is fine in the CLI runtime.
   559	    const id = valueAfter('--id') || `adhoc-${taskType}-${Date.now().toString(36)}`;
   560	    const adhocSpec = { taskType, brief };
   561	    console.error(`hopper-dispatch v${VERSION} — ad-hoc ${taskType} -> ${vendor || '(routed)'} [id: ${id}]`);
   562	    if (args.includes('--background')) {
   563	      await runBackgroundDispatch(hopperDir, id, { adapterOpts, vendorOverride: vendor, adhocSpec });
   564	    } else {
   565	      await runDispatch(hopperDir, id, { write: true, adapterOpts, vendorOverride: vendor, adhocSpec });
   566	    }
   567	    return;
   568	  }
   569	
   570	  // Multi-vendor SWARM (panel): fan the SAME qualitative brief out to N vendors, each as
   571	  // its own ad-hoc background dispatch (N single-spawns, no retry; one per vendor). The
   572	  // vendor selection + per-vendor config is confirmed HOST-side (/hopper:swarm); this just
   573	  // executes the confirmed list with shared config (per-vendor config → N `--adhoc` calls).
   574	  if (args.includes('--swarm')) {
   575	    const valueAfter = (flag) => {
   576	      const i = args.indexOf(flag);
   577	      return i !== -1 && args[i + 1] !== undefined && !args[i + 1].startsWith('-') ? args[i + 1] : null;
   578	    };
   579	    const taskType = valueAfter('--task-type');
   580	    const brief = valueAfter('--brief');
   581	    const vendorsArg = valueAfter('--vendors');
   582	    if (!taskType || !brief || !vendorsArg) {
   583	      console.error('Error: --swarm requires --task-type <t> --brief "<text>" --vendors <v1,v2,...>.');
   584	      console.error('Example: hopper-dispatch --swarm --task-type code-review-acceptance --brief "review X" --vendors codex,grok,claude');
   585	      process.exit(2);
   586	    }
   587	    let panels;
   588	    try {
   589	      panels = planSwarm({ taskType, brief, vendors: vendorsArg, idBase: valueAfter('--id-base') });
   590	    } catch (err) {
   591	      console.error(`Error: ${err.message}`);
   592	      process.exit(2);
   593	    }
   594	    const known = listAdapters();
   595	    for (const p of panels) {
   596	      if (!known.includes(p.vendor)) {
   597	        console.error(`Error: unknown vendor '${p.vendor}' in --vendors. Registered: ${known.join(', ')}`);
   598	        process.exit(2);
   599	      }
   600	    }
   601	    // --swarm FORCES the read-only/qualitative default — never let --sandbox upgrade a
   602	    // panel to write access (that would defeat the read-only premise the task-type gate
   603	    // exists for). Reject it; use per-vendor --adhoc if a different sandbox is truly needed.
   604	    if (valueAfter('--sandbox')) {
   605	      console.error('Error: --swarm forces read-only (qualitative panel); --sandbox is not allowed.');
   606	      process.exit(2);
   607	    }
   608	    const adapterOpts = {};
   609	    try {
   610	      if (args.includes('--web-search')) adapterOpts.webSearch = true;
   611	      const reasoning = valueAfter('--reasoning'); if (reasoning) { validateReasoning(reasoning); adapterOpts.reasoning = reasoning; }
   612	      // Still no shared --model: one model id genuinely IS meaningless across
   613	      // diverse vendors. But that objection is now ANSWERED rather than left to
   614	      // the caller — each panelist resolves its OWN model through the normal
   615	      // per-vendor chain (AGENTS.md `Approved Vendors` → `Default model`, else
   616	      // the adapter's declared hopper default; see resolveAdapterOptsForTask).
   617	      //
   618	      // Until 2026-08-11 nothing filled that gap, so every panelist inherited
   619	      // whatever its vendor CLI felt like and the handoff recorded
   620	      // `requested_selector: null`: a live panel ran pi on gpt-5.5 out of
   621	      // ~/.pi/agent/settings.json while every other pi dispatch used the 5.6
   622	      // line, and grok recorded `observed_models_json: []`. Reject an explicit
   623	      // --model here so the per-vendor resolution is not overridden by a single
   624	      // id that can only be right for one panelist.
   625	      if (valueAfter('--model')) {
   626	        console.error('Error: --swarm takes no --model — one model id cannot be right for diverse vendors.');
   627	        console.error('Each panelist resolves its own model: `.hopper/AGENTS.md` → `## Approved Vendors` → `Default model`,');
   628	        console.error('else that adapter\'s hopper default (see `hopper-dispatch --capabilities <vendor>`).');
   629	        console.error('For a one-off per-vendor model, dispatch that vendor with `--adhoc --vendor <v> --model <m>`.');
   630	        process.exit(2);
   631	      }
   632	    } catch (err) {
   633	      console.error(`Error: ${err.message}`);
   634	      process.exit(2);
   635	    }
   636	    console.error(`hopper-dispatch v${VERSION} — swarm ${taskType}: ${panels.length} panelists [${panels.map((p) => p.vendor).join(', ')}]`);
   637	    const launched = [];
   638	    const failed = [];
   639	    for (const p of panels) {
   640	      try {
   641	        await runBackgroundDispatch(hopperDir, p.id, { adapterOpts, vendorOverride: p.vendor, adhocSpec: { taskType: p.taskType, brief: p.brief }, exitOnError: false });
   642	        launched.push(p.id);
   643	      } catch (err) {
   644	        // Failure isolation: one panelist failing (auth / host==vendor / etc.) must not abort
   645	        // the rest of the panel. Collect + continue; already-launched panelists keep running.
   646	        failed.push({ vendor: p.vendor, error: err.message });
   647	        console.error(`  panelist ${p.vendor} did not launch: ${err.message}`);
   648	      }
   649	    }
   650	    console.log('');
   651	    if (launched.length) {
   652	      console.log('SWARM_IDS: ' + launched.join(' '));
   653	      // Watching a PANEL used to have no affordance at all — the closing lines
   654	      // said only how to COLLECT — so hosts hand-rolled pollers out of `wc -c`
   655	      // on the raw log and `ps -p <pid>` (which cannot even see a Windows PID).
   656	      // Name the real surfaces; a panel is watched one panelist at a time.
   657	      console.log('Watch a panelist:  hopper-dispatch --progress <id>   (or --watch <id> to follow until it exits)');
   658	      console.log('Panel at a glance: hopper-dispatch --jobs            (ALIVE column; do not poll the raw log by hand)');
   659	      console.log('Collect each panelist with: hopper-dispatch --result <id> --full');
   660	    }
   661	    if (failed.length) {
   662	      console.log(`Not launched (${failed.length}/${panels.length}): ${failed.map((f) => f.vendor).join(', ')}`);
   663	    }
   664	    if (!launched.length) process.exit(1);
   665	    return;
   666	  }
   667	
   668	  if (args.includes('--status')) {
   669	    const summary = await getStatus(hopperDir);
   670	    console.log(`hopper-dispatch v${VERSION} — queue summary`);
   920	    if (arg.startsWith('-')) {
   921	      throw new Error(`Unknown flag "${arg}". Bare flags allowed: ${[...BARE_FLAGS].join(', ')}. Value flags: ${[...VALUE_FLAGS].join(', ')}.`);
   922	    }
   923	
   924	    // First non-flag, non-value arg becomes task-id
   925	    if (taskId === null) {
   926	      taskId = arg;
   927	    } else {
   928	      throw new Error(`Unexpected extra positional argument "${arg}" (task-id already set to "${taskId}").`);
   929	    }
   930	  }
   931	
   932	  return { taskId, bareFlags, valueFlags };
   933	}
   934	
   935	async function runResolve(hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride = null) {
   936	  try {
   937	    // docs/archive/ISSUES.md#resolve-ignores-vendor-override fix: thread vendorOverride straight
   938	    // into resolveDispatch — the SAME `vendorOverride || resolveVendor(...)`
   939	    // formula and the SAME assertVendorApproved(agentsData, vendor) gate
   940	    // (cli/src/dispatch.js:72,77) that a real dispatch applies, not a
   941	    // reimplementation. An override is therefore checked against
   942	    // .hopper/AGENTS.md's Approved Vendors table here exactly as it would be at
   943	    // real dispatch time (E_APPROVED_VENDORS_SECTION_MISSING / E_VENDOR_NOT_APPROVED
   944	    // surface via the catch below, same as runDispatch/runBackgroundDispatch).
   945	    const result = await resolveDispatch({ hopperDir, taskId, vendorOverride });
   946	
   947	    // Host != vendor family isomorphism guard — runDispatch/runBackgroundDispatch
   948	    // apply this immediately after resolveDispatch, before any adapter/spawn work.
   949	    // It is pure (env lookup + a static family table; no subprocess), so a dry
   950	    // run can and must apply it too — otherwise --resolve could report a Vendor:
   951	    // line a real dispatch would actually refuse. A same-family collision throws
   952	    // (caught below, same as a real dispatch would abort); an unrecognized host
   953	    // is non-blocking and surfaces as a notice line, matching runDispatch.
   954	    const { id: hostVendor } = resolveHostIdentity();
   955	    const hostCheck = validateHostVendorSeparation(hostVendor, result.vendor);
   956	
   957	    console.log(`hopper-dispatch v${VERSION} — resolved dispatch for ${taskId}`);
   958	    console.log(`  Task-type: ${result.task.taskType}`);
   959	    console.log(`  Vendor:    ${result.vendor}${vendorOverride ? ' (--vendor override)' : ''}`);
   960	    if (hostCheck.notice) console.log(`  notice:    ${hostCheck.notice}`);
   961	    // Where the task content came from, when it did NOT come from a detailed
   962	    // leader-tasklist.md section. This diagnostic used to be smuggled into the
   963	    // vendor prompt as the task spec itself; it is an OPERATOR notice.
   964	    if (result.specNotice) console.log(`  notice:    ${result.specNotice}`);
   965	    console.log(`  Brief:     ${result.task.brief}`);
   966	    console.log(`  Composed prompt length: ${result.composedPrompt.length} chars`);
   967	    if (process.env.HOPPER_DEBUG) {
   968	      console.log('');
   969	      console.log('=== COMPOSED PROMPT ===');
   970	      console.log(result.composedPrompt);
   971	      console.log('=== END PROMPT ===');
   972	    }
   973	
   974	    // HOPPER-1: --resolve used to print the resolved vendor verbatim even when
   975	    // it is NOT a registered adapter — e.g. a model id like `gpt-5.5` mistakenly
   976	    // placed in the queue.md Vendor column or the AGENTS.md preference table.
   977	    // The failure then surfaced much later at dispatch as the opaque
   978	    // "No vendor adapter registered for 'gpt-5.5'". Catch it at resolve time and
   979	    // distinguish a model-in-the-Vendor-column mistake from a typo'd adapter.
   980	    const known = listAdapters();

   349	/**
   350	 * Status summary for --status command.
   351	 *
   352	 * @param {string} hopperDir
   353	 */
   354	export async function getStatus(hopperDir) {
   355	  const queuePath = join(hopperDir, 'queue.md');
   356	  const tasks = await parseQueue(queuePath);
   357	  return summarizeQueue(tasks);
